The Sky Today on Thursday, November 12: An open cluster in Perseus

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

Looking for a sky event this week? Check out our full Sky This Week column.

The constellation Perseus is famous for its bright Double Cluster, but these two open clusters aren’t the only groups of young stars in the Hero worth viewing. Today, let’s shift our gaze to the western portion of this constellation, near its border with Andromeda, to check out M34, a mid-5th-magnitude open cluster containing about 100 stars.

Sitting 5.2° west-northwest of 2nd-magnitude Algol (Beta [β] Persei), M34 spans about half a degree, roughly the size of the Full Moon. In physical space, astronomers believe the group spans about 14 light-years and sits 1,400 light-years away. It’s best seen under lower magnifications, such as in binoculars or small amateur scopes (4 inches or so). You’ll spot several dozen stars brighter than 12th magnitude, with its brightest suns spread throughout the cluster.

Sunrise: 6:42 A.M.Sunset: 4:46 P.M.Moonrise: 10:08 A.M.Moonset: 6:57 P.M.Moon Phase: Waxing crescent (14%)*Times for sunrise, sunset, moonrise, and moonset are given in local time from 40° N 90° W. The Moon’s illumination is given at 10 P.M. local time from the same location.

Alison Klesman is senior editor of Astronomy magazine. She holds a Ph.D. in astronomy and has studied a variety of topics, from minor planets to supermassive black holes.

How to Build a Resilient Personal Investment Plan

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

Home » Op-ed » How to Build a Resilient Personal Investment Plan

Build a resilient personal investment plan with clear goals, diversified assets, and practical risk management. Learn how to assess costs, liquidity, and alternative investments before committing your money.

A sound investment plan ties your financial goals, time horizon, risk tolerance, and available resources together into one coherent strategy. Without that connection, even well-intentioned investment decisions can end up working against you. Diversification—spreading investments across and within asset classes rather than concentrating in a single holding—remains one of the most consistently cited principles in personal finance education. Some investors, while researching ways to diversify, come across physical assets such as gold bars as one example of exposure outside traditional stocks and bonds. This isn’t a recommendation; it’s simply an illustration of the kind of asset category investors may compare when evaluating a broader plan. This article focuses on the decision-making principles, risk management practices, and due diligence steps that apply regardless of which specific assets you ultimately choose.

Before selecting any investment, figure out what the money is actually for. Emergency savings, a short-term purchase, retirement, education funding, and long-term wealth building each come with different requirements for accessibility and acceptable volatility. Money you’ll need within the next year or two generally calls for safer, more liquid holdings. Money earmarked for goals decades away can typically absorb more fluctuation in exchange for growth potential.

It helps to separate two related but distinct concepts: risk tolerance, which is how much market fluctuation you can emotionally stomach, and risk capacity, which is your financial ability to absorb losses without jeopardizing your goals. Income stability, outstanding debt, the size of your emergency reserve, number of dependents, and investment time horizon all factor into risk capacity. Writing down your goals and constraints before researching specific investments helps prevent decisions driven by excitement or fear.

Several types of risk deserve separate attention: volatility (short-term price swings), permanent loss of capital, liquidity risk (difficulty converting an asset to cash without taking a discount), inflation risk (the erosion of purchasing power), and credit risk (a counterparty’s inability to meet its obligations).

Asset allocation refers to dividing a portfolio among categories such as stocks, bonds, cash, real estate, and alternative assets. Each category tends to respond differently to economic conditions, which is why diversification can reduce concentration risk—though it can’t eliminate the possibility of loss altogether. A realistic target allocation, one you can stick with through market stress rather than abandon at the first sign of volatility, matters more than a theoretically optimal one you can’t maintain. Rebalancing—periodically returning a portfolio to its intended mix—is a discipline rooted in preserving your chosen risk profile, not in predicting short-term market movements.

One educational approach organizes a portfolio into broad functions: liquidity for near-term needs, stability for capital preservation, growth for long-term objectives, and diversification for assets that may behave differently from your core holdings. This framework is illustrative only and shouldn’t be treated as a universal formula.

Key takeaway: A resilient portfolio begins with goals and constraints—not with chasing the latest fashionable investment.

Alternative assets are sometimes considered for diversification, potential inflation resilience, or reduced dependence on traditional financial assets. Gold, for instance, is occasionally discussed as a diversifier with no direct credit exposure, though its price can still fluctuate meaningfully. Historical behavior should never be mistaken for a guarantee of future performance.

Exposure to an asset like gold can take several forms: physical ownership, exchange-traded products, mining-company shares, or futures and other derivatives. Each form carries its own market, operational, counterparty, liquidity, and regulatory characteristics. Owning a physical asset is fundamentally different from owning a financial product whose value is merely linked to that asset’s price.

Understanding the economics of physical ownership requires a few plain-language definitions: the spot price is the current market price for immediate delivery; the purchase premium is the amount charged above spot price; the resale spread is the difference between what you pay and what you’d receive upon selling; storage and insurance costs are ongoing expenses tied to safekeeping; and liquidity describes how quickly and efficiently you can convert the asset back to cash.

Looking only at the quoted purchase price overlooks the full picture. The total cost of ownership includes premiums, storage, insurance, and eventual resale spreads. Before purchasing, it’s worth checking:

Be wary of unsolicited offers, urgency-based sales tactics, guaranteed-return claims, or requests for unusual payment methods. Compare multiple independent sources and hold on to receipts and ownership records. Product specifications, legal treatment, and tax rules vary by jurisdiction and change over time, so verify details through authoritative sources and qualified professionals.

Physical assets come with a storage decision to make: home storage, bank or institutional safe-deposit arrangements, or professional third-party vaulting. Each option involves trade-offs in privacy, access, security, insurance costs, and estate planning complexity. Physical possession introduces practical risks—theft, loss, damage—that don’t apply in the same way to securities held in a regulated brokerage account. Maintaining a detailed inventory and folding relevant assets into your estate documents is worthwhile. The safest approach depends on individual circumstances, local laws, and available insurance coverage.

Consider two hypothetical investors. The first spends time comparing dealers, calculates her total ownership costs, and limits her purchase to a small percentage of her portfolio in line with a written plan. The second, swayed by a high-pressure phone pitch promising rapid appreciation, commits a large sum without comparing alternatives or understanding the resale terms. The contrast says something about process quality, not about any specific asset.

Revisit your plan after major life changes—a new job, marriage, inheritance, retirement, or a significant shift in debt. Market movements alone don’t necessarily call for a strategic overhaul. Scheduled reviews should cover goal progress, emergency reserves, asset allocation, fees and ownership costs, and beneficiary or estate information. For decisions involving significant assets or complex obligations, consulting a qualified financial, tax, or legal professional is a wise move.

Before adding an alternative asset, consider:

A durable investment plan rests on clear objectives, genuine diversification, reasonable costs, adequate liquidity, and informed risk management—not on any single product. Physical precious metals may suit some financial plans, but they’re not automatically appropriate for every investor. Compare alternatives objectively, verify claims independently, and steer clear of decisions made under pressure. Take time to review your overall portfolio, write down the intended role of each holding, and seek qualified advice whenever a decision exceeds your own expertise.

This article is educational in nature and does not constitute personalized financial, tax, or legal advice. Consult a qualified professional before making investment decisions.

Can one intense workout per week keep you healthy? Here’s the science.

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

Published Nov 12, 2026 8:53 AM EST

The so-called “weekend warrior” exercise approach offers an enticing trade: skip the workouts all week, and cram them into one or two days instead. It sounded great to me, until I realized it could turn my hard-earned “Thank God it’s Friday” into “Oh God, it’s Friday.”

Still, for people who genuinely lack the time to exercise during the workweek, this approach could offer real convenience—and real health benefits.

A recent study in Hong Kong has supported that idea, finding that people who packed all their weekly exercise into one high-intensity interval training (HIIT) session lost about as much body fat as those who spread the same workout across three sessions.

So, is doing all your exercise in one session really just as good as spreading it out? The answer depends on your goals and overall health. But according to Dr. Keith Diaz, an exercise physiologist at Columbia University, even infrequent exercise has real value.

“You don’t have to be a daily gym goer to reap the benefits,” he says. “Some exercise, even if it’s just one time a week, provides health benefits.”

The study, published in Nature Communications, spent 16 weeks following 315 adults who were overweight or had central obesity. The researchers split the participants into three groups, with one doing a single 75-minute HIIT session per week, and another spreading this exercise across three weekly sessions.

“They had participants do three 25-minute HIIT sessions of 4 minutes hard and 3 minutes easy,” Diaz says. “There was 15 to 30 minutes between HIIT sessions, plus a 5-minute warm-up and 5-minute cool-down.” The once-weekly group did all three of those 25-minute blocks in a single visit; the second group spread them into three visits across the week, one block per visit. A third group, the controls, did not exercise, and instead attended health education classes.

By the end of the 16 weeks, both HIIT groups had lost more body fat than the control group. Specifically, the once-weekly group lost about 1.75 pounds more than the control group, and the group that exercised three times a week lost 2.2 pounds more. Waist circumference, which is a measure of central obesity, also improved significantly in both exercise groups compared to control.

“These were relatively modest reductions in fat,” Diaz says. Neither group reported any exercise-related injuries.

The researchers measured much more than fat loss. They also tracked cardiorespiratory fitness, which is a measure of how efficiently the body uses oxygen during exercise, and a strong predictor of long-term health.

By this measure, the two workout groups did not perform equally. The group that exercised three times a week achieved roughly an 11 percent improvement in cardiorespiratory fitness, compared with an approximate 6 percent improvement in the once-weekly group. Diaz has a theory for why this might be the case.

“Physiologically, the more frequently you stress your body with exercise, the more it will adapt and yield changes to your heart, blood vessels, and muscles,” he says.

So, if fat loss is your main priority, a “weekend warrior” workout might be enough. However, Diaz believes the study actually makes a case for spreading exercise across the week “if your focus is on being fitter and generally being more healthy.”

So who does stand to benefit from squeezing a week’s worth of exercise into one session? According to Diaz, the ideal “weekend warrior” is a healthy person who, because of the demands of their daily lives, might not get around to exercising otherwise.

“This approach is best suited for generally healthy individuals who are short on time during the week and struggle to exercise because of their busy weekday schedule,” he says.

However, some people with preexisting health conditions might not see the most benefit from this highly consolidated regimen. In particular, Diaz says people with diabetes or hypertension should try to be physically active every day.

“Exercise lowers blood sugar and blood pressure for 12 to 24 hours afterward,” he says. “So, the weekend warrior approach would not be ideally suitable for individuals with diabetes or hypertension, who need these regular reductions in blood sugar or blood pressure.” Diaz adds that people with heart conditions should discuss once-weekly HIIT with their doctors before starting.

Do rage workouts really help to blow off steam?

Should you work out on an empty stomach? Fitness experts weigh in.

How much exercise do I need to stay healthy?

Although the once-weekly approach has undeniable appeal and might truly benefit some busy folks, Diaz notes that it’s not exactly the magical quick fix some headlines would suggest.

“I think the headlines around one day of exercise a week melting belly fat are misleading,” he says. “Yes, it’s one day of exercise, but it’s a lot in one day.”

Although the exercise itself takes 75 minutes in total, adding time to warm up and rest between the sessions means that the single weekly workout takes almost three hours to complete. And it’s not melting the fat away.

Despite that deflating reality check, Diaz sees real potential in this approach when healthy people use it appropriately.

“This study adds further support that some exercise, even if just on the weekend, is better than none,” he says. “So if the weekend is the only time you have to exercise, this could be an option for you.”

Joe Hart pinpoints ‘unbelievable’ Alisson Becker trait which makes him ‘one of the best’

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

Joe Hart has proclaimed that Alisson Becker will ‘forever be regarded as one of the best’ goalkeepers to have graced the Premier League.

Liverpool’s number 1 has been rightly acknowledged as one of the foremost players in his position in world football since his move to Anfield in 2018 and has been pivotal towards the trophies won by the Reds in that time.

He produced one of his greatest LFC performances in the victory at Crystal Palace last weekend, with his tally of six saves including a world-class reaction stop from Daichi Kamada which left the Eagles attacker astounded.

In an interview with The Athletic, Hart lavished praise on Alisson for the consistency he’s shown throughout his time in England, and especially his prowess in one-on-saves.

The ex-Manchester City stopper said of the 34-year-old: “One of the best to do it in the Premier League. What I love is his simplicity. He’s not necessarily eye-catching in the way he moves, but it’s the way he deals with everything.

“He makes massive saves. His one-on-one technique is unbelievable, and he has made it his own. He very rarely gets beaten one-on-one, which in this Premier League is incredible because you’re up against some of the best strikers in the world.

“He’s struggled a bit with injuries lately, but I think he’ll forever be regarded as one of the best to play the position.”

Want more Empire of the Kop coverage? Add us as a preferred source on Google to your favourites list for news you can trust

Last weekend, Alisson produced a perfect example of the one-on-one ability that Hart pinpointed by thwarting Yeremy Pino when the Crystal Palace forward was through on goal, a significant intervention at a time that Liverpool were 2-0 up.

When it comes to ‘massive saves’, it mightn’t quite be in the same category as his legendary stoppage-time heroics against Napoli in December 2018 when our Champions League campaign was in the balance, but it showed that the 34-year-old remains a world-class goalkeeper.

His outstanding display at Selhurst Park came after a couple of costly misjudgements in defeats to Arsenal and Fenerbahce, which shows the Brazilian’s strength of character to put those unfortunate moments behind him and come up trumps for his team.

Alisson’s agent Ze Maria revealed in recent days that talks have begun over a prospective contract renewal at Liverpool, with the Reds’ number 1 having said after the win at Palace that he’d like to stay at Anfield beyond the expiry of his current deal next summer.

More heroics like those he produced in south London four days ago, and an extension to his existing terms should be a no-brainer for sporting director Julian Ward.

Is the Crypto Market Walking Into a Bull Trap? Bitcoin ETF Records Five Straight Days of Outflow

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

Ahmed Barakat is a journalist and copywriter based in Georgia with a growing focus on blockchain technology, DeFi, AI, privacy, digital assets, and fintech innovation.

The CryptoNews editorial team is composed of seasoned writers specializing in cryptocurrency and blockchain technology. Their expertise ensures comprehensive, accurate, and insightful content for...

Bitcoin’s latest recovery attempt is running into a serious problem: institutional investors are pulling money out of the market. U.S. spot Bitcoin ETF recorded five consecutive days of net outflows, shedding $1.44 billion between November 5 and November 11.

Bitcoin is now trading near $73,500, struggling to reclaim the $75,000 level. Bitcoin ETF outflow streak raises a bigger question: Is the crypto market walking into another bull trap?

The timing is particularly concerning. Bitcoin briefly reclaimed $80,000 on November 4 before reversing sharply the following day. Crypto has since struggled to regain momentum, leaving us questioning buyers’ decisions and whether we are stepping in too early.

The latest Bitcoin ETF withdrawals included $430.2 million on November 5, followed by another $411.9 million on November 6. Outflows continued on November 9, 10, and 11.

BlackRock’s iShares Bitcoin Trust (IBIT) accounted for most of the selling pressure. The fund alone recorded $269.7 million in net outflows yesterday.

The latest Bitcoin ETF outflow streak shows that institutional demand is struggling to support the market’s recovery. Five consecutive sessions of withdrawals have removed $1.44 billion from U.S. spot Bitcoin funds. BlackRock’s IBIT accounted for three-quarters of that amount.

However, institutional demand has not disappeared entirely. Morgan Stanley’s Bitcoin ETF attracted $9 million yesterday, offering a small counterpoint to the ETF-wide selling.

Glassnode’s latest Week 46 report adds another warning. Bitcoin has fallen below the short-term holder cost basis of $75,600. This leaves recent buyers sitting on unrealized losses.

The research firm also points to shrinking stablecoin supply and continued ETF selling. Both developments display that fresh capital is struggling to enter the market.

This creates an uncomfortable setup for anyone buying the latest dip. A short-lived recovery could attract retail investors before another wave of selling pushes prices lower.

Bitcoin’s derivatives market offers another piece of the puzzle. BTC futures open interest remains above $50 billion, while funding rates remain positive. This number somehow paints a clear picture: leveraged traders are still paying to maintain bullish positions.

However, leverage has already started coming out of the market. Open interest has declined, and much of the long-side leverage near current prices has been flushed.

That creates two possible outcomes. If spot demand returns, reduced leverage could help Bitcoin recover without triggering another major liquidation cascade. Alternatively, another failed breakout could leave remaining leveraged buyers exposed to further downside.

For now, $75,600 is the important recovery threshold. Reclaiming that level would put Bitcoin back above the short-term holder cost basis and improve market sentiment.

A grinding climb toward $80,000 would offer stronger evidence that buyers are regaining control, but losing the $72,000 area could expose BTC to another test of $70,000.

The bull trap argument also has a counterpoint. There is accumulation among two large wallet cohorts, while selling momentum has started weakening. This shows that some investors are already positioning for a recovery.

The market does not need another dramatic crash to trap investors. Sometimes, all it takes is a convincing bounce that runs out of buyers.

Former Rangers manager quits role after mirroring disastrous Sunderland record in 2958 day low

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

Former Rangers head coach Michael Beale has quit as manager of Belgian Pro League side Zulte Waregem just weeks after offering fans a refund following a heavy away defeat.

According to reports, Beale has resigned after just 177 days at the Elindus Arena, after the 4-1 defeat to Sint-Truidense saw them fall to their sixth consecutive loss, matching their worst run of results since October 7, 2018. The ex-Rangers man leaves them just four points clear of the relegation zone.

Did you know with a Digital Subscription to The Scotsman, you can get unlimited access to the website including our premium content, as well as benefiting from fewer ads, loyalty rewards and much more.

His first managerial role since being sacked by Sunderland almost three years ago, Beale is understood to have fallen on his sword due to a breakdown in his relationship with senior figures over the club’s direction and strategy. The 46-year-old’s time started brightly as they went unbeaten in their opening five games. However, their form quickly unravelled, and they have been without a win since September 6.

"I will sit down with the board during the international break and we will put our heads together,” said Beale at the weekend. "Will I still be here when we return for our next match? A lot depends on how the discussions go. Last summer, we made a decision to concentrate on young players, but we have to accept that this group now needs help. We can't deny we are in trouble."

While the club are yet to officially confirm Beale’s departure, numerous reports say he has informed the club of his decision to depart. Bizarrely, his poor run of results means that he leaves his role with an identical record to his ill-fated spell at the Stadium of Light, where he also recorded four wins, two draws, and six losses, resulting in a 33.3% win rate after 12 games at both clubs.

Appointed by Zulte Waregem on May 19, he offered to refund fans’ ticket and travel costs following a heavy 3-0 defeat to La Louviere last month, approaching supporters at the final whistle to inform them of the reimbursement in person.

The ex-Ibrox boss first linked up with Rangers as part of the coaching staff during Steven Gerrard’s three-and-a-half-year reign in charge of the club, working as Rangers’ first-team coach as they went unbeaten to win the Scottish Premiership title in the 2020/21 season. He joined the former Liverpool icon in departing the club shortly afterwards, with the pair moving to Aston Villa in November 2021.

After leaving the Midlands upon Gerrard’s sacking shortly afterwards, Beale moved into his first managerial role with EFL Championship side Queens Park Rangers the following summer, but was poached by Rangers as a replacement for Giovanni van Bronckhorst just five months into his spell at Loftus Road, and was named the Ibrox club’s 18th permanent manager in their history in November 2022.

He was unable to replicate his previous success at Rangers though, and struggled in the main role at Ibrox. A mixed start to his tenure, saw him win record eight wins, two draws and four defeats. A 3-1 home defeat to Aberdeen sealed his fate though, with the club opting to bring an end to his time in the dugout after a third defeat in seven games. Appointed as manager of Sunderland 15 months later, his time on Wearside was a disaster, and the club parted ways with him after just nine weeks in the role, where he was succeeded by Regis Le Bris.

Netflix to Release Documentary ‘Russell Brand: Open Secret’

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

Netflix will release a new documentary, Russell Brand: Open Secret, on Nov. 13. The film, directed by Olivia Isaacs, “charts the story of Russell Brand’s meteoric rise and steep fall following allegations of sexual offenses.”

According to a press release, Open Secret features inside voices and archive footage that offer insight into Brand’s life and the investigation run by U.K. publications the Times and the Sunday Times and their reporters Rosamund Urwin and Charlotte Wace. A short teaser for the film calls Brand a “shapeshifter” and a “grifter,” and explains that the journalists initially didn’t realize their investigation would lead to criminal charges.

Brand was found guilty of one count of rape and three counts of sexual assault on Nov. 5 following a trial in London. He was found not guilty on two additional counts of rape, as well as one count of attempted indecent assault. The allegations against him were tied to claims made by six women between 1999 and 2009. The actor and comedian was sentenced to 10 years in prison.

Police began investigating Brand in 2023 after multiple women came forward with allegations of assault and abuse in a Channel 4 documentary and the Sunday Times report. Prosecutors first filed five charges against Brand in April 2025, while two additional charges were brought in December.

Following the trial, Brand’s attorney Ian Winter confronted Urwin outside the courtroom, telling her “I hope you are pleased with yourself.” Urwin responded, “I’m pleased justice has been done,” to which Winter replied, “Well, it wasn’t. You fucked it.” Winter later confirmed he had referred himself to the Bar Standards Board.

Urwin explained on a Times podcast that she approached Winter after the verdict because her name had been mentioned during the trial. “I thought he’s talked about me a lot, I’ll go and introduce myself and that is a legitimate thing to do,” she said. “I was surprised to be confronted and spoken to in that way.”

The bulk of Brand’s sentence is tied to the rape charge, for which the judge sentenced him to nine years in prison. Meanwhile, each of the three assault charges carried their own penalty: nine months, 18 months, and eight months. The assault sentences will be served concurrently, but consecutively with the sentence for the rape charge. Brand will eventually be eligible for parole, but only after serving two-thirds of his sentence. That means he will spend at least seven years behind bars, and, if granted parole, would have to serve the remainder of his sentence under supervision and abiding by specific rules.

The Metropolitan Police are continuing to investigate Brand after several people contacted them with information during and after the trial. “Detectives are assessing this information as part of their ongoing enquiries,” a spokesperson told the BBC.

Is Your Console Ready for Grand Theft Auto 6? Here’s What You Can Do to Make Room

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

Our gifting experts are obsessively searching for holiday gifts that are unique, thoughtful and suitable for any budget.

Our expert, award-winning staff selects the products we cover and rigorously researches and tests our top picks. If you buy through our links, we may earn a commission.

Deleting old games isn’t the only option you have to make space for GTA 6.

Grand Theft Auto 6 hits shelves on Nov. 19, 2026, and if you preordered the game, you can preload it on your PlayStation 5 or Xbox Series X/S now. Preloading ensures you can quickly boot the game up when it’s released.

Rockstar Games revealed that GTA 6 is 212 GB, which will take up about half of my Xbox Series S’s storage space. The company announced that GTA 6 is available to stream via Xbox Cloud Gaming without downloading it to your console. However, cloud streaming GTA 6 might affect the game’s performance and relies heavily on a strong internet connection. To avoid those issues, I plan on downloading the game.

If you’re like me and are excited to play GTA 6 but don’t have much storage space, you have two options: delete games or upgrade your console’s hard drive. Here’s what you should know about both options.

The simplest and cheapest way to free up space on your console for Grand Theft Auto 6 is to delete files and other games, which is what I’ll be doing. Uninstalling games is a good choice for folks who plan on spending a lot of time with GTA 6 and don’t intend to juggle different games. Rob Nelson, who oversees Rockstar North with Aaron Garbut, told The New York Times that he had an 80-hour playthrough of the game in February, so you can easily stay immersed in Grand Theft Auto’s state of Leonida.

You may not need to delete much – uninstalling a game like Call of Duty alone could free up nearly 315 GB, giving you enough digital storage for GTA 6. Since deleting files is almost instantaneous, you can quickly reclaim hard drive space. Because you don’t need to buy additional storage, you’ll also save a lot of money – storage prices are through the roof at the moment.

Ultimately, deleting games is a great option if you don’t want to spend any additional money or you don’t mind redownloading games later.

If you plan on hopping between GTA 6 and other games, or you don’t want to delete many of your downloaded titles, you can upgrade your console’s storage. Buying storage is more expensive than deleting files from your console, but benefit from being able to seamlessly hop between Grand Theft Auto 6 and Call of Duty or Madden.

You can use internal or external storage for your console, but you might have to move files between your internal and external storage to launch games. An internal hard drive lets you seamlessly play games. Xbox consoles rely on proprietary expansion cards from Seagate or SanDisk that plug into your console for additional storage. The PlayStation 5 uses M.2 solid-state drives, which are installed alongside the PS5’s built-in storage, and CNET’s David Carnoy recommends the Lexar NM 790 as a fast SSD with a straightforward installation process.

There shouldn’t be any performance, quality or load-time differences between running games from installed storage expansion devices versus your console’s internal storage. Both expansion cards and SSDs can be expensive, especially when compared with deleting games.

You could also use an external hard drive to expand your storage, which can be cheaper than buying expansion cards and internal SSD upgrades – you may even have a compatible USB external drive sitting around that you can use. However, there are caveats around what games can and can’t be played directly from external HDDs.

For Xbox consoles, some older Xbox, Xbox 360 and Xbox One titles can be stored and played from external HDDs. But games optimized for Series X/S consoles can only be stored on HDDs and must be moved back to your internal drive to play. Similarly, the PlayStation 5 only lets you store PS5 games on external HDDs, while you can store and run PS4 games from external drives.

Therefore, using an external HDD requires playing digital musical chairs, moving current-gen games back and forth between your external hard drive and internal console storage in order to play them. The LaCie Rugged SSD4 earned a CNET Editors’ Choice Award and could be a good option. If its price tag scares you, the Western Digital P10 offers more storage at a more economical price point.

Rockstar announced that GTA 6 will be available to stream on the Xbox consoles – no download required – via Xbox Cloud Gaming, with some quality and time limitations. You’ll need to purchase either an Xbox Game Pass plan or Cloud Gaming hours. Folks who don’t want to delete games or upgrade their console storage can stream GTA 6, but you’ll need an Xbox One or Series X/S console, won’t get the highest visual quality and will have to pay for more playtime hours if they run out. Streaming GTA 6 could help budget-conscious gamers who don’t want to upgrade from Xbox One consoles or who don’t have the allotted storage space on their devices.

Normally, you can stream many games on PCs, mobile devices and TVs or streaming devices using Xbox Cloud Gaming or Game Pass, but Grand Theft Auto 6 is limited to the Xbox One, Series S and Series X. Additionally, GTA 6 streaming quality is capped at Series S graphical settings. So if you have a Series X, consider deleting games or upgrading your storage to run the game locally with better graphics.

While you’ll need to install the full game on your console first, remote play for Xbox and PlayStation consoles lets you stream GTA 6 to other devices, like your phone, tablet, streaming device or TV.

If you’re willing to pay a little more for the convenience of having multiple games ready to play at a moment’s notice, Xbox expansion cards or an internal SSD for a PS5 are your best options. If you want a cheaper option and don’t mind moving game files around, an external hard drive could be for you. If you have Xbox Game Pass or Xbox Cloud Gaming, you could stream the game to your console without needing more storage space. However, the cheapest option is to delete games from your internal storage and redownload them later, which can be time-consuming.

You only have a few days left until Rockstar releases GTA 6, so you’d better prepare your digital storage now, or you might not be immediately able to play. I plan on deleting old games to make room on my console. You won’t catch me slipping on Nov. 19.

Füllkrug Admission Raises Uncomfortable Question About West Ham’s Striker Recruitment

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

Ordinarily, I wouldn’t be in too much of a hurry to pay attention to what Niclas Füllkrug says about West Ham. However, the fact that he currently tops the Bundesliga goalscoring charts with nine goals in nine games for Werder Bremen does lend some credibility to his latest comments.

Speaking while on international duty with Germany, Füllkrug has suggested that a lack of service was partly responsible for his struggles at West Ham and AC Milan. In an interview published by the German Football Association, he explained that his Bremen teammates actively look to find him with the ball, something he felt was missing at his previous two clubs.

The German striker cost West Ham £27 million when he arrived from Borussia Dortmund, and there’s little doubt in my mind that the Hammers failed to have a proper plan for incorporating him into the team. However, Füllkrug must also take his share of the blame because his first season was blighted by injury, and he also spoke about feeling homesick.

The truth is that Füllkrug was never really on the pitch for long enough for us to discover whether anything could have come from his transfer. His constant fitness problems made it almost impossible to establish any consistency, and he has now acknowledged that staying fit was the key issue during his time abroad.

That said, there is little in West Ham’s performances under Julen Lopetegui, Graham Potter or Nuno Espírito Santo that suggests we would have been able to get the best out of what is clearly a talented striker.

There may well be something in his assertion that he wasn’t given a decent supply line when he did play for West Ham. Indeed, we’ve previously looked at how Bremen have built their attacking play around Füllkrug, with teammates providing the service and support he so often appeared to lack in East London.

As for his fitness, I’m afraid that’s very much on him. West Ham have consistently demonstrated over the past few years that their medical team, physiotherapists and fitness staff have generally done a good job of keeping players available.

Füllkrug’s injury record was a significant part of the problem, and whilst it’s perfectly reasonable for him to question the service he received, it doesn’t explain why he spent so much of his West Ham career unavailable for selection.

Nevertheless, watching a striker who managed just three goals for West Ham now leading the Bundesliga scoring charts is bound to raise questions about the original recruitment decision. The fact that the club are still dealing with the financial consequences of his move only adds to the frustration.

Füllkrug has rediscovered his goalscoring touch at Bremen, and good luck to him. But I suspect the truth about his West Ham failure lies somewhere between the club not knowing how to use him and the player simply not being fit enough to give himself a proper chance.

Custom Ad Algorithms Are Helping Schibsted Media Win Budgets Back From Meta

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

For years, ad budgets have been shifting from the open web and independent publishers to walled garden platforms like Meta.

But recently, that shift has begun to reverse a bit.

Nearly two thirds – 63.9% – of advertisers have moved mobile budgets from walled gardens to independent partners in the past year, according to a July survey of 166 brands and agencies by eMarketer and Datum Intelligence. Only 1.2% of the survey respondents shifted their budgets the opposite way.

That being said, eMarketer projects Meta, Google and Amazon will attract 62.3% of worldwide digital ad spend this year, with Meta topping Google in ad revenue for the first time.

Still, publishers around the world, including Norwegian-based broadcaster and TV news conglomerate Schibsted Media Group, are trying to take advantage of any shift away from Meta and the other walled gardens they can get.

Schibsted is working with Nodals AI, a London-based company that creates custom ad optimization algorithms for publishers in Europe and the US, to help court brands who’ve been wooed by Meta’s own optimization tools, Christer Ljones, director of ad data capabilities at Schibsted Marketing Services, told AdExchanger.

Schibsted owns VG, the largest digital news publisher in Norway, boasting about 2.4 million daily visitors. It also owns Aftonbladet, one of the largest digital news publishers in Sweden, which claims 3.6 million daily visitors. And it acquired Sweden’s TV4 and Finland’s MTV Oy – two of the largest TV broadcasters in those countries – from Swedish telecommunications group Telia last year.

These properties, combined with the rest of Schibsted’s local newspaper network, gives the company roughly 50% audience reach in the geos it covers, Ljones said.

To put Schibsted’s audience size in perspective compared to US publishers, its largest properties, VG and Aftonbladet, have wider reach than the Los Angeles Times, which attracts a combined weekly online and print audience of 4.4 million, or about 11% of California’s population.

VG and Aftonbladet make most of their money from advertising, with the local publications more reliant on subscriptions, he said. Online display ads and branded content account for most of the company’s ad revenue, with a roughly even split between the two.

Like other large publishers with strong reach concentrated in specific markets, Schibsted has long pitched advertisers on working with the company directly to take advantage of its market saturation and its deep knowledge of its audiences, Ljones said. The direct ad business comprises roughly two thirds of Schibsted’s ad revenue, with programmatic making up the remaining third.

But audience reach isn’t the only thing that matters to performance-minded brands. The walled gardens have won advertisers over with AI-powered campaign optimization features like Meta’s Advantage+, Ljones said, and it’s been hard for open-web publishers to keep up on the ad tech side.

It’s also been harder for open web publishers to compete when it comes to direct sales, said Nodals Founder and CEO Aly Nurmohamed.

“Advertisers have been trained by Meta to just care about ‘I put money in and I see how much money comes out,’” Nurmohamed said. “So the problem for Schibsted was advertisers were saying, ‘I don’t want to buy on a CPM, and you’re really expensive relative to Meta. Here’s what I’m doing with Meta. Can you replicate it?’”

Schibsted has always seen Meta as a competitor and not a “partner,” Ljones said. While some publishers experienced short-term gains from working with Meta in the past, he added, the platform is no longer shy about cutting publishers out of its ad business.

That dynamic is part of the reason Schibsted has been leading the charge against Meta’s planned terms of service update. The update would have required all publishers active on Meta platforms to share data for training the company’s AI models – essentially, training the optimization tools that are helping Meta take publishers’ portion of ad revenue.

Schibsted, along with 180 other publishers, joined the Norwegian Media Businesses’ Association in suing Meta over its TOS update at the end of October. Within two days of the lawsuit being filed, the Oslo District Court ruled in the publishers’ favor, imposing a freeze on Meta’s TOS update in Norway until its legality could be investigated. Legal decisions like this one likely prompted Meta to pause its TOS update across all regions earlier this month.

But legal battles are just one way Schibsted is fighting against Meta’s market dominance. Custom algorithms, like those Nodals creates, are another weapon in its arsenal.

Schibsted sees these custom algorithms as a way to remind advertisers of its effectiveness in driving performance and brand awareness by using similar tools to those offered by Meta, Ljones said.

Nodals pitches itself as a solution for publishers to create their own Meta-esque optimization engines.

And Schibsted could be in a strong position to win back some of the business it’s lost to Meta. It’s maintained a direct relationship with its audience through its owned and operated properties, even as other publishers struggle with AI search stealing their traffic, according to Ljones. As a result, it’s only reliant on search for a single-digit percentage of the traffic on its largest sites.

That direct relationship with its audiences gives Schibsted the first-party data insights it needs to compete with Meta’s campaign optimization capabilities, Nurmohamed said.

“We take their data and feed it into their own models, collecting everything they know about what their audiences are, any logged-in data they have, how far you scroll down the page, how often you click on content or ads,” he said. “All of that goes into our model, and then you can have a powerful prediction on who is likely to click and buy on a particular advertiser.”

Plus, because the publisher owns the optimization algorithm and the data that powers it, Nodals’ solutions shouldn’t be affected by Apple’s recent efforts to crack down on third-party audience data vendors and ID graphs, Nurmohamed said.

Schibsted first tested Nodal’s tech last summer and has been incorporating it into its direct sales efforts since those successful tests, Ljones said. It’s been building a new product line of direct-sold native ad formats with AI optimization built in, priced on a cost-per-click basis, rather than on a CPM model.

The new products are resonating with some of Schibsted’s traditional advertisers, such as grocery stores serving the Norway and Sweden markets, who are looking for a mix of performance and brand awareness from their ad campaigns, he said. And, while Schibsted “won’t be necessarily as good as Meta on average” when it comes to driving performance, “you don’t need to beat them on the average. You just need to beat them with meaningful scale at the margin.”

For example, Schibsted has found success pitching advertisers that have been pouring more money into Meta lately but seeing diminishing returns, Ljones said.

“We get feedback from advertisers that they’ve seen their Meta ROI, their conversions, flatten out, or maybe even decline slightly,” he said. “So, the overall idea that we see initially working is, ‘Let’s take 10% of whatever you spend on Meta, and you can probably keep most of your conversions and get more [from us].”

Ljones feels confident that Schibsted can build on those margins going forward.

“We’re in an early phase, but we’re seeing the right results,” he said. “For us, that’s taking new budgets from existing clients, defending existing budgets in a few cases, but also addressing clients we basically lost.”

AI is taking over the media and tech industries in the name of simplification, and marketers are pining for provable campaign outcomes, including from TV. These shifts are also causing significant change in Agency Holdco Land, according to Angela Steele, the first chief client officer for WPP Media US.

No major retail media player has taken as circumspect an approach to ad revenue as Shopify. But the company has recently taken its next important step with a product called Campaign Autopilot.

OpenAI’s head of monetization, Benji Shomair makes the case for ads as a way to bring ChatGPT to more people, with user trust taking priority over advertiser demands.

In Q3, The Trade Desk beat the admittedly pessimistic revenue forecast it set for itself earlier this year. But growth has stalled.

Viant is doubling down on CTV with its $20 million Waymark acquisition, betting that AI-generated ads can bring more advertisers to streaming TV.

TIME is using AI agents to draft proposals and troubleshoot campaigns – but not to replace the human relationships that drive ad sales.

AdExchanger is where marketers, agencies, publishers and tech companies go for the latest information on the trends that are transforming digital media and marketing, from data, privacy, identity and AI to commerce, CTV, measurement and mobile.

Opinion: Liverpool transfer U-turn will be massive for wonderkid

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

Liverpool have already been through a lot of ups and downs this season.

They've lost three games across all competitions and, while their defeats against Manchester City and Arsenal can be accepted, their 3-2 loss against Fenerbahce was terrible.

There's no way that the Turkish Super Lig side should have even had a chance of beating Liverpool but, thanks to their poor performance, they were humbled.

These outings have caused many different transfer links to emerge from Anfield and now, the club look likely to make a transfer U-turn that could be hugely beneficial to one of their wonderkids.

Alexis Mac Allister is looking more likely to stay at Liverpool

Alexis Mac Allister has undoubtedly been the best midfielder among Liverpool's ranks this season.

But, before the campaign kicked off, that was far from the case.

Last term, he was massively underperforming which led fans to question whether he was even worth keeping around with some even begging a team such as Real Madrid to make a move for him.

Obviously, this didn't happen and, although at the end of summer this felt like a bit of a disappointment, fans will be thankful that he stayed put.

However, with his contract expiring in June 2028, a decision needs to be made on his future soon.

And, although most of the reports to emerge from Liverpool have shared that the Reds didn't want to extend his stay, a new update from journalist Ismael Mahmoud says otherwise.

"We will hear good news in the next 10 days I guess," Mahmoud wrote in response to a quote from Steven Gerrard who stated that he thinks Mac Allister should be offered a new contract.

Alexis Mac Allister staying with Liverpool is great news for Trey Nyoni

As mentioned, Mac Allister has been extremely important for Liverpool this season.

Without him, the Reds would have been way worse off and would have probably ended up even further down the Premier League table than they currently are.

But, not only has Mac Allister had a positive impact on the way things have gone at Liverpool but, he's also brought Trey Nyoni on leaps and bounds.

The 19-year-old was on the verge of joining Swansea on a season-long loan in the summer but, thanks to Curtis Jones' desire to leave and Iraola's trust in youngsters, he was asked to stay.

This is arguably one of the most important decisions that Liverpool made this season as, even though the midfielder is only 19 years old, every time he's been used, he's managed to control the game.

And, for one reason or another, whenever he's played alongside Mac Allister, the best version of the Englishman emerges.

So, keeping the Argentinian around could genuinely be the best thing for Nyoni which, in the long-run, will massively benefit Liverpool.

This is the kind of Republican that I am.”

NASA’s Hubble Offers Cosmic Sampler of Targets for Roman

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

A gallery of gravitational lenses captured by NASA’s Hubble Space Telescope showcases the diversity of this cosmic phenomenon and lays the groundwork for follow-up observations by the new Nancy Grace Roman Space Telescope.

Gravitational lensing, first predicted by Albert Einstein, occurs when the gravity of a massive foreground object, such as a galaxy or cluster of galaxies, bends, warps, and magnifies the light from a more distant background object. The effect is similar to that of a giant lens in space that allows astronomers to view objects much farther away than any telescope could see on its own.

The lenses in this image formed from the precise alignment of a distant background galaxy with the mass of a foreground galaxy, creating distorted and magnified images of the background galaxy that appear as arcs around the foreground galaxy.

Astronomers will use these observations of a large and varied population of gravitational lenses to construct lens models that will help determine how much mass is in the lensing system’s foreground galaxy – and potentially prove or dispel a theory about dark matter. Astronomers study the amount of distortion of the lensed background galaxies to determine the quantity and distribution of mass in the foreground galaxy, including its invisible dark matter.

Hubble views the cosmos primarily in visible and ultraviolet light with some infrared coverage, providing a comprehensive, high-resolution view of individual objects. Roman sees primarily infrared light and some visible, over a much larger field of view to create panoramas of the universe. While Hubble gets a better view of individual lenses, Roman will be able to capture more lenses with its wider field-of-view. If Roman finds a particularly interesting lens, researchers may use Hubble to take follow-up observations using its visible and UV prowess for individual objects.

With Hubble's high-resolution imaging and precise models, researchers should also be able to detect small imperfections in these gravitationally lensed galaxy arcs that would indicate the presence of small clumps of dark matter. Researchers could use these previously undetected clumps to confirm the cold dark matter theory, which predicts the existence of many small dark matter clumps of between a million to a billion solar masses. In contrast, the dark matter halo of our Milky Way galaxy is around 1.3 trillion solar masses.

By studying Hubble lens images in detail, researchers intend to build a super-intelligence tool to efficiently search similar arcs in the upcoming Roman images. The Hubble images are expected to both yield their own results on dark matter and pave the way for analysis of Roman’s larger sample of lenses.

The unique lenses in this image display some of the different characteristics researchers will see as they collect a diverse and numerous assortment of lenses to test in their models. Panel A is an example of an arc-counterarc configuration, in which the lensed background galaxy appears as a duplicated pair of arcs. The lensed galaxy is being magnified by a pair of foreground galaxies whose combined mass bends the light of the background galaxy around them.

Panel F is an Einstein Ring, in which the alignment of the background galaxy and approximately spherical mass of the foreground lens are so perfectly situated that the lensed galaxy almost fully encircles the foreground galaxy. Panel I shows a quadruply lensed galaxy, in which the background galaxy is replicated four times, appearing as smeared dots around the foreground galaxy. Panel C also shows a quadruply lensed galaxy, with double knots that are most likely strong star-forming regions in the background galaxy.

Some of the magnified and warped background galaxies appear blue and some appear red. These bluish arcs indicate young stars, while reddish arcs indicate older stars. Roman and Hubble will explore these differences to better understand how galaxies evolved earlier in the universe.

Most of the foreground lensing galaxies are elliptical galaxies, which are older, denser, and more compact. Lensed images of background galaxies only occur when the mass density of the foreground galaxy reaches a critical point.

But not all of these foreground galaxies are ellipticals, and researchers can use these differences to explore the evolutionary paths taken by these galaxies. In panel G, the foreground galaxy appears elongated, which may be true or a trick of perspective, and in panels B and F, the foreground galaxies appear to have spiral arms.

Panel D’s foreground galaxy has a visible dust lane, which is rare in a lensing galaxy and the kind of detail that Hubble’s sharp, visible-light vision excels at revealing. The lensed background galaxy could be an Einstein Ring, with the dust lane “breaking” our view of the ring. Because infrared light is less obscured by dust, future Roman infrared observations could help determine whether the arcs form a more complete Einstein ring.

These strong lenses were first detected with The Dark Energy Spectroscopic Instrument at Kitt Peak. Hubble subsequently observed them, and Roman will take further observations. Images like these are an example of the kinds of collaborative efforts that routinely take place between NASA’s Great Observatories to build a comprehensive picture of the cosmos. As Roman joins the family of large space observatories that includes Hubble, the Chandra X-ray Observatory, and the James Webb Space Telescope, a new window opens onto the universe, revealing sights previously hidden while expanding our cosmic knowledge for all of humanity.

Claire AndreoliNASA's Goddard Space Flight Center, Greenbelt, MDclaire.andreoli@nasa.gov

Since its 1990 launch, the Hubble Space Telescope has changed our fundamental understanding of the universe.

Hubble and Webb observations complement each other, providing us with a broad view of the universe.

Spring Returns to Ezequiel’s Cottage Garden in Argentina

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

While many of our gardens are settling in for winter, Ezeiza, Argentina, is enjoying the last stretch of spring before summer arrives in December. Today we’re heading back to visit Ezequiel. We’ve followed his cottage garden through the seasons over the years, including another beautiful spring visit. After some difficult weather, he is back with another update as his garden fills with flowers ahead of summer.

I hope you are all doing very well during the autumn in the North. In my country, following the harsh and troublesome “El Niño” weather phenomenon, the long-awaited and much-loved spring has finally arrived in my cottage garden in Ezeiza.

Plants and flowers are just beginning to emerge, a promise of the summer that will be in full swing by December here in the Southern Hemisphere. Penstemons, salvias, irises, verbenas, dahlias, bulbines, daisies, and my lemon tree… Everything has begun to stir, grow, and grace us with beautiful blossoms, lovely scents, and fragrances.

Thank you, as always, for being there! And thank you for sharing in the joy of my garden.

From Argentina, sending you my best regards and a big hug.

A look into Ezequiel’s cottage garden in Ezeiza, Argentina.

‘Hot Lips’ salvia (Salvia microphylla ‘Hot Lips’, Zones 7–10) in bloom.

Louisiana iris (Iris × louisiana, Zones 4–9) showing off.

Purple coneflowers (Echinacea purpurea, Zones 3–9) are flowering next to the large sweep of lavender (Lavandula cvs., Zones 5–10) along the side of the driveway. I can practically smell the lavender looking at this photo!

This massive lantana (Lantana camara, Zones 8–11) stopped me in my tracks! Here in the North, we usually grow lantana as an annual because it isn’t winter hardy. Seeing what this tender perennial can become in a warmer climate is pretty incredible.

Bearded iris (Iris cvs., Zones 3–9), California poppy (Eschscholzia californica, Zones 8–10), and orange bulbine (Bulbine frutescens, Zones 9–11) make a colorful combination in this perennial bed.

Another view of the massive lantana, this time with a gorgeous specimen crimson bottlebrush (Callistemon citrinus, Zones 8–11) in full bloom.

Another view of the backyard. This photo has me wondering just how far it extends. I’m so curious about the layout of this garden—it seems like there’s something new to discover just about everywhere!

Thank you for bringing us back to your beautiful garden, Ezequiel! It’s always such a treat to see what’s growing in your corner of the world, especially while our seasons here in the North are moving in the opposite direction.

Harry Kane for England - the only non-negotiable in Thomas Tuchel's Three Lions squad

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

For really the first time in his international career, Jordan Pickford has genuine competition for his place as the first-choice goalkeeper, and Leeds United's James Trafford will be hoping for the chance to ramp it up with an appearance either at home to Croatia on Thursday, or in Spain on Sunday. One more cap seems likely, two feels like a push.

But his club-mate Dominic Calvert-Lewin just has to accept he was born at the wrong time – anyone who wants to be England's centre-forward in this era can forget about it unless their name happens to be Harry Edward Kane.

Did you know with a Digital subscription to Yorkshire Post, you can get access to all of our premium content, as well as benefiting from fewer ads, loyalty rewards and much more.

Tuchel has already confirmed the 33-year-old will be leading his team out at Wembley – anything else would be cruel.

Getting Kane to miss internationals is a tough job in itself. The number of dead rubbers or easy victories he has featured in is very high but no one should criticise him for being hungry to wear the Three Lions, or even to add to a prodigious goal tally which currently stands at 91.

Even putting him on the bench against the team he scored twice against in October's astonishing 7-0 away win with only a point needed to qualify for the quarter-finals would be an act of cruelty.

For starters: Thursday is the night Kane will be presented with the Ballon d'Or as the best player in Europe – and by extension the world – in the season just gone. It will also be the night he breaks Peter Shilton's record of 125 caps, a record he set in 1989 and extended over the next year.

Yes, internationals are more ten-a-penny than in Shilton's day with competitions like the Nations League springing up and FIFA's greed expanding World Cups beyond what is sensible.

But Wayne Rooney, David Beckham, Steven Gerrard, Ashley Cole and Frank Lampard all made it to three figures this century without any going past a goalkeeper who might have played more often had he not been part of a Pickford/Trafford-style rotation with Ray Clemence early in his England career.

And to those of us of a certain age, 49 goals felt like the glass ceiling for England players.

Now it is hard to know where Kane will stop. A ton feels likely.

Bobby Charlton hit his 49th goal in his 101st appearance, in 1970, but could not bring up his half-century in five more caps.

Gary Lineker looked a cert for 50 at one stage, but got stuck in the mud when Graham Taylor foolishly lost faith with him in 1992, missing a penalty for 49 in what proved to be his final appearance, then being controversially substituted for Alan Smith in a European Championship defeat to hosts Sweden.

Alan Shearer only made it to 30 before his international career came to a premature end but when Michael Owen burst onto the scene as an 18-year-old, the record looked under threat again. His time with England fizzled out too, hamstring injuries cutting the country's last Ballon d'Or winner short at 89 caps and 40 goals by the age of 28.

Finally, in 2015, 29-year-old Rooney made it to 50 on the night Kane got his third goal. Thirteen more caps reaped just three more.

Kane has obliterated that, and looks a far more complete player than even then one who set a new record with his 54th goal in 2023.

Then he was a Tottenham Hotspur player, a brilliant No 9, but since moving to Bayern Munich he has developed into an exceptional "false nine" – spending as much time in midfield as up front, and even helping out defensively. He is one of the best midfielders around.

Kane was edging in that direction in his late 20s, but his time in Germany has taken him to a new level.

“I actually don't see a better player, but I see a player that believes he can win,” argued Gareth Southgate, the manager who took him to two European Championship finals and 98 caps. “That's happened to him in the last month alone would have given him another boost of confidence.

“If you sit there and you think: 'My peers, experts, the world has voted me as the best player in the world,' my goodness, how good is that going to make you feel when you've been a player that people said can't even make it as a professional?”

Like Rooney, Kane could extend his career by dropping deeper on the field, a chink of light for those centre-forwards, like Sheffield-born Calvert-Lewin craving a look-in.

His recently-signed new Bayern contract is due to keep him playing for almost three more years. At the rate he has been winning them lately, that would be 36 more caps.

The lessons of Lineker, Shearer, Owen and Rooney is that the end can come abruptly and unexpectedly. Despite what his hairline might have you believe, Charlton was only 32 when he retired.

Whether he makes it to 100 goals or not, Kane is a special talent England should be forever grateful for.

Through NASA’s Lens: Capturing Space Shuttle Endeavour’s Move to LA

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

As a new exhibit opens showcasing NASA’s space shuttle Endeavour, NASA photographers look back at the final mission. These photographers documented the iconic images shared around the world on Sept. 21, 2012, while millions watched space shuttle Endeavour fly on top of the agency’s 747 Shuttle Carrier Aircraft for the last time.

Endeavour’s new home at the California Science Center in Los Angeles will reopen the weekend of Nov. 13, displayed upright in launch position, ready to inspire the next generation of space explorers.

“Chasing Endeavour’s final ferry flight over Southern California was, without a doubt, a leading highlight of my 37-year career at NASA,” said Lori Losey, lead videographer at NASA’s Armstrong Flight Research Center in Edwards, California. “I advocated for two chase aircraft to be used in the LA area so we could dedicate one aircraft to photo and one to video. It was this beautiful dance of alternating altitudes and swapping positions between landmarks to maximize both of us capturing the legendary imagery.”

Two photographers and one videographer, all based at NASA Armstrong, had a leading hand in preparing the Endeavour’s flight to Los Angeles, and in deciding where else the aircraft would fly around California on its final journey. Agency pilots planned the route to fly across some of California’s most iconic landmarks and the imagery team strategized about how to best capture the images.

“The most fun thing about this project was seeing the excitement of the people on the ground as we flew over the different landmarks,” said Jim Ross, NASA Armstrong’s lead photographer. “People in the Los Angeles area really came out and supported the Endeavour flyover. We saw so many supporters at Griffith Park and Disneyland.”

Photographer Carla Thomas said seeing the standing-room-only crowd below the California State Capitol in Sacramento filled her with pride. After joining the agency in the wake of the Challenger tragedy, she reflected on the opportunity to document NASA’s return to flight.

Endeavour was escorted by NASA Armstrong’s F/A-18s and an F-15, which carried the photo and video teams in the back seats. Like many flight efforts, this journey took a significant amount of preplanning with the California Science Center, as well as groups like the Federal Aviation Administration, the Los Angeles International Airport, and NASA’s Johnson Space Center in Houston.

“The planning was my favorite part of this effort — it was fun and exciting to plan the shots,” Thomas said. “I flew the entire route with the LAPD (Los Angeles Police Department) in one of their helicopters. We had to make sure this was going to work and find any obstacles. Planning for the Hollywood sign was challenging, because it’s on the side of a hill with power lines and radio antennas, but we worked through these issues in the planning process.”

The space shuttle’s flight path included other California landmarks: Dodger Stadium, the Getty Center, the Los Angeles Memorial Coliseum, NASA’s Ames Research Center in Silicon Valley, the Golden Gate Bridge, NASA’s Jet Propulsion Laboratory in Pasadena, and the Santa Monica Pier.

Endeavour helped begin assembly of the International Space Station by delivering its first components to orbit and went on to complete 25 missions. The space shuttle will soon be seen, along with some of the images from the final flight, in its permanent home inside the new Samuel Oschin Air and Space Center at the California Science Center in Downtown Los Angeles.

Space Shuttle Endeavour To Make Historic Final Ferry Flight

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

Since the Trump administration took office in January 2025, it has taken or proposed at least 580 actions that directly threaten the environment, climate, and human health. NRDC is tracking the mounting hazard and harm.

As soon as the second Trump administration took office in January 2025, we witnessed an immediate and unprecedented attack on our environment and public health. This includes a rollback of crucial environmental safeguards, the repeal of bedrock environmental and health laws, the delay of critical protections for endangered species, and attacks on well-established science and scientists.

At NRDC, we believe it is critical to keep track of the administration’s relentless efforts to undo decades of progress. Here, we are tracking on all that the Trump administration has done—and continues to do—to harm our environment and public health. This is a living document that will be continuously updated as NRDC analyzes the administration’s actions and as new threats emerge.

Martin-Pichon a pensé à nous

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

Après s'être longuement confié à Poteaux Carrés, l'ancien Vert et actuel milieu de terrain de Rumilly-Vallières Cyril Martin-Pichon (à droite sur la photo, à côté de Yann M'Vila et Neven Subotic) s'exprime dans la dernière édition du Dauphiné Libéré avant d'affronter son club formateur ce dimanche après-midi à Annecy. Extraits.

"J’aurais pu rester plus longtemps à Saint-Etienne mais j’ai toujours privilégié le jeu, le plaisir, l’enchaînement des matches. Ma dernière saison à l'ASSE [2019-2020, ndp2], j’aurais aimé être prêté mais ça ne s’est pas fait. J’étais le capitaine de la réserve, mais on m’avait dit à la trêve hivernale qu’il fallait faire jouer la génération suivante. J’aurais pu plus m’accrocher pour faire une carrière en Ligue 2 ou Ligue 3, mais j’ai fait les choix qui étaient humainement les meilleurs pour moi. Je sais que j’aurais pu me faire plus mal pour jouer plus haut mais c’est un risque de quitter un endroit où on se sent bien et je ne suis pas assez armé, pas assez égoïste pour arriver dans un club et ne penser qu’à moi. C’est vrai que je me dis parfois que "l’ancien foot" me correspondait mieux. Je n’ai pas de regrets car j’ai toujours été heureux dans ma vie."

Patrick Stewart's Widow Sunny Ozell Breaks Silence After His Death at 86

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

Patrick Stewart’s widow, musician Sunny Ozell, has broken her silence on the legendary actor’s death at age 86.

“Today marks two weeks since Patrick passed, and the grief continues to come in waves,” Ozell, 47, wrote via Instagram on Wednesday, November 10.

Ozell continued, “But I’ve also been experiencing such deep, profound gratitude. We left nothing unsaid. He knew I loved him, and I know he loved me. Patrick and I truly, authentically saw one another, and we amplified each other’s best qualities.”

“We shared nearly two decades together, and we filled those years with so much *living*,” Ozell wrote. “I’ve been overwhelmed by the public response to Patrick’s death, and while I’m not at all surprised that he touched so many, I’m sincerely moved by the outpouring of sadness and loss.”

She concluded, “Thank you all for your condolences, they mean so, so much to me. My heart had to grow to contain my love for Patrick, and he changed me for the better, forever.”

Ozell married the X-Men and Star Trek: The Next Generation actor in 2013. He was previously married to Sheila Falconer — with whom he shares son Daniel, 59, and daughter Sophie, 54 — from 1966 to 1990 and Wendy Neuss from 2000 to 2003.

Stewart’s spokesperson confirmed his October 28 death in a statement.

“[Patrick experienced] a brief and poignant journey with Alzheimer’s disease and Lewy body dementia,” spokesperson Kelly Bush Novak said. “He has been held in the warm embrace of family and friends, surrounded by live music, prayer, tears, laughter and the kind of profound emotion that only he could inspire after a lifetime of devotion to us all. We will forever cherish the peaceful ease and reflection on his face, an enduring reminder of his invincible grace.”

Before his death, Stewart lamented his estrangement from his adult children, Daniel and Sophie. He told the Irish Independent in 2023 that the separation had caused him “significant grief.”

“Given that I’m separated from my children now, we don’t have relationships, they have become very important,” he said at the time. “I never thought that this would happen, but both my children are in their 50s, they’re not children anymore, they’re adults. And my relationship with them is practically nonexistent.”

He continued, “There’s not a day that goes by that I’m not pained by the memory of my children, who they were and by a sense of responsibility that they became who they are in part because of me and my influence or lack of influence on them.”

Ryan Porteous on his Scotland exile, Steve Clarke relationship and ‘things that people don’t see’

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

When the goal that clinched Scotland’s World Cup return went in, Ryan Porteous might once have hoped he’d be celebrating with his Scottish teammates.

Instead, he was thousands of miles away at what he terms a “practice facility” in California. Jumping on top of him were not Scotland international teammates but those from Los Angeles FC. “When Kenny McLean scored (against Denmark) from the halfway line, I’ve got half the team in LA shouting and screaming for Scotland!” the defender recalled. “It was brilliant."

Did you know with a Digital Subscription to The Scotsman, you can get unlimited access to the website including our premium content, as well as benefiting from fewer ads, loyalty rewards and much more.

Due to the time difference, he was able to sit and watch most of the qualifiers after training. “Obviously being in the mode of being a fan is only good up to a point," he noted. "You want to be involved, so I’m delighted to be back again.”

Porteous has played just one minute plus injury time for the national team – against Greece last year – since being sent off in the first half of the opening match of Euro 2024 against Germany.

The received wisdom is he was being punished for his rash, penalty-conceding challenge on Ilkay Gundogan in Munich, with Scotland already 2-0 down. The roof subsequently caved in and Scotland were beaten 5-1.

The pitiful sight of Porteous waiting for the trap door to open at the Allianz Arena - there is a hatch leading to the underground players’ tunnel - after he was given his marching orders is still burned in the mind. For him as much as anyone.

The first surprise on seeing him again is his lack of bitterness towards Steve Clarke, the manager who initially handed him his chance before seemingly ghosting him. In actual fact they continued to enjoy a healthy relationship and were in contact throughout the time he was being overlooked.

“That happens in football, it’s just one of those things,” Porteous reflected. “I’ve had a career where I’m up and down, I’m left and right, you just need to bounce back from it.

“But it was a fantastic experience for me to be part of that run we went on. It helped me grow massively.

“I don’t look at it as being unfair. Me and the old gaffer (Clarke) spoke regularly. We had loads of conversations, both when I was in and out of the squad. Those are the things that people obviously don’t see.

“They maybe think Steve Clarke is the type of person who wouldn’t phone you or text you. But he did. We spoke loads to be honest. He was really honest with me.

"I texted him when he left the role and he messaged back eager to see me get back in the squad. Everyone knows Steve is a manager who, if he picks a squad and they do well for them and they get to a tournament, he’s loyal.”

Porteous perhaps didn’t help himself. Clarke finally played him again against Greece in a Nations League play-off in March last year with Scotland desperately defending a 1-0 lead given to them by Scott McTominay’s first-half penalty. Sent on after 89 minutes to replace Anthony Ralston, he could realistically have seen red once more with a clumsy challenge.

He wasn’t picked again by Clarke although he was placed on stand-by for the World Cup. Already in the States, having joined LAFC from Watford, he would have made for a handy replacement.

In the event he wasn’t required. He still flew cross country to Boston to join in the fun when his schedule – the Major League Soccer season had only paused for the World Cup - allowed. He didn’t have tickets but he did at least taste the Tartan Army invasion of Fenway Park, home of the Boston Red Sox. “I had a few days off at one point and I went out to Boston,” he recalled. "I didn’t manage to go to the games, but I saw my friends and family who were over and went to the baseball as well. Being in that fan environment, it just gives you that extra little bit of hunger to get back.”

And now he is back, at the behest of new manager Sebastien Pocognoli rather than Clarke. Porteous, who was in action as recently as Sunday in the 2-1 loss to Seattle Sounders, has replaced the injured Stephen Welsh. He has been joined by fellow late call-up David Bates, who returns from an even longer exile of seven years following an injury to Luis Binks.

The delight is written across Porteous’ tanned face. “It’s very hot (out there),” he says. “They are actually choking for a bit of rain just now.”

He jokes that he might just be the first person ever to move from Dalkeith, where he grew up and stayed while with Hibs, to LA. There were stop-offs at the equally unprepossessing Watford and Preston, where he was on loan, in between times.

Even the accent has had a glow-up. “I’ve had to change it so that people can understand me over there,” he said. “For the first couple of months, everyone was asking me to repeat myself.”

One thing hasn’t changed. He laments that he is still losing his hair. “I used to blame the wind at East Mains (Hibs’ training ground) for losing my hair, but it’s still falling out over in LA even without the wind!” he smiled.

He hopes the fans will recognise his old qualities minus the impetuousness that has bedevilled his performances in the past, stretching back to his time at Hibs. It’s still slightly surreal to see him listed in the same team as Son Heung-min, who left Spurs to join LAFC for an MLS record fee of £20 million two summers ago.

“Son is brilliant,” he said. “Some high-profile guys like him maybe get a reputation for going to America and taking it easy.

“But he has been unbelievable. He plays basically every single game and gives absolutely 100 per cent. He’s one of the best in every game and every training session. He’s a really top, top guy as well.”

Report: Amazing news, Trent Alexander-Arnold regen is wanted by Liverpool

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

Liverpool have found their next Trent Alexander-Arnold and he’s playing in France. The Reds have taken a step towards a transfer.

Liverpool lost Trent Alexander-Arnold in 2025 and have struggled badly to replace him. That's not a good thing when the player was such an integral part of a title-winning team.

The Reds relied heavily on his passing through the lines, let alone his direct assists, and his absence has been incredibly notable. They tried Jeremie Frimpong in the role, of course, moving away from a passer to more of a runner but it hasn't worked out.

Ronald Araujo now holds down the role but he's not an attacking threat. Instead, Liverpool just look more solid on the flank - but they're notably less dynamic.

Finding a suitable replacement is difficult if not impossible, of course, as Alexander-Arnold is such a unique player. So unique, in fact, that only Liverpool were able to figure out how to use him. Neither England nor Real Madrid have had much luck with it.

Incredibly, though, we think the Reds might have found another one.
