Sydney’s Powerhouse Parramatta Museum and Bratislava’s Winter Harbour Redevelopment: This Week’s Review

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

This week's stories focus on cultural architecture and the ways museums, art institutions, and public venues are being designed, expanded, and adapted. Across different cities, new projects bring together exhibition spaces, educational programmes, and areas for public activity, while existing buildings take on new cultural uses. From Sydney and Sharjah to Miami and Dubai, the projects reflect a range of approaches to creating and extending spaces for art and culture, with attention to their programmes, audiences, and connections to the surrounding urban environment.

This week's stories focus on cultural architecture and the ways museums, art institutions, and public venues are being designed, expanded, and adapted. Across different cities, new projects bring together exhibition spaces, educational programmes, and areas for public activity, while existing buildings take on new cultural uses. From Sydney and Sharjah to Miami and Dubai, the projects reflect a range of approaches to creating and extending spaces for art and culture, with attention to their programmes, audiences, and connections to the surrounding urban environment.

Cultural Institutions and New Public Destinations

The opening of major cultural institutions this week highlights the role of architecture in expanding access to art, education, and public programming while contributing to broader urban development. In Sydney, Moreau Kusunoki and Genton's Powerhouse Parramatta Museum has opened as a major cultural destination in Western Sydney, bringing together exhibition spaces, educational facilities, and public programs within one of Australia's largest cultural infrastructure projects. In Sharjah, the third edition of the Sharjah Architecture Triennial has announced its opening programme, bringing together 35 participants through performances, film screenings, lectures, and discussions under the theme Architecture Otherwise: Building Civic Infrastructure for Collective Futures. Meanwhile, in New Mexico, Multistudio and Design Plus Architects' Literacy Institute is preparing to open in December, introducing an educational environment that combines flexible learning spaces, recreational facilities, and community areas to accommodate different approaches to literacy and learning.

The opening of major cultural institutions this week highlights the role of architecture in expanding access to art, education, and public programming while contributing to broader urban development. In Sydney, Moreau Kusunoki and Genton's Powerhouse Parramatta Museum has opened as a major cultural destination in Western Sydney, bringing together exhibition spaces, educational facilities, and public programs within one of Australia's largest cultural infrastructure projects. Meanwhile, in New Mexico, Multistudio and Design Plus Architects' Literacy Institute is preparing to open in December, introducing an educational environment that combines flexible learning spaces, recreational facilities, and community areas to accommodate different approaches to literacy and learning.

Related Article "Earth Craft" at the U.S. Pavilion Will Trace the Work of Builders at the 2027 Venice Architecture Biennale

Expanding Museums and Reimagining Existing Buildings

New cultural projects and institutional expansions also demonstrate how architecture can support evolving forms of public engagement. In Miami, Gensler has been appointed to lead the renovation and expansion of the Institute of Contemporary Art (ICA Miami)'s second building in the Design District. Scheduled to open in late 2027, the project will add 30,000 square feet of space, doubling the museum's existing facilities through eight galleries, educational spaces, and a flexible venue for public programming. In Dubai, Johannesburg- and London-based architect Sumayya Vally of Counterspace has unveiled plans for the Elie Khouri Art Foundation's permanent home, her first permanent project in the United Arab Emirates. Scheduled to open in late 2028, the project will transform an existing villa in Jumeirah 1 into a contemporary art institution, combining exhibition galleries, educational spaces, pavilions, and gardens while retaining elements of the property's domestic character.

KCAP Places Second in Competition for Bratislava's Winter Harbour Redevelopment

KCAP has received second place in the international competition for the redevelopment of Bratislava's Winter Harbour, organized by the Metropolitan Institute of Bratislava on behalf of the Port Authority and the city. Selected from 123 entries, the proposal transforms the 45-hectare former industrial harbour into a mixed-use district with approximately 10,400 homes, 5,800 workplaces, and three new parks, while reopening five kilometres of the Danube waterfront to public access. Titled The Living Waterfront, the masterplan retains the site's industrial heritage, including historic cranes, railway tracks, warehouses, and the 1904 pumping station, integrating them into new public spaces and cultural facilities. The proposal also prioritizes pedestrian and cycling routes and introduces a flood-responsive landscape strategy that allows 17.7 hectares of the waterfront to remain floodable. First place was awarded to Hosoya Schaefer Architects, with Cobe taking third place.

Populous to Develop Masterplan for London Stadium and Surrounding Area

London Stadium has appointed Populous as Lead Consultant to develop a spatial strategy for the stadium and its surrounding site within Queen Elizabeth Olympic Park. The study will examine potential leisure and mixed-use developments, including entertainment facilities, hotels, hospitality, conference spaces, and food and beverage venues. It will also consider improvements to the site's public spaces, landscape, and connections with the surrounding park and neighbourhoods. Building on feasibility work completed in 2024, the commission will bring together design, planning, operational, and commercial considerations, with Populous working alongside the London Borough of Newham to establish a framework for potential future development.

MoMA, Centre Pompidou, and M+ Establish International Cultural Alliance

The Museum of Modern Art (MoMA) in New York, Centre Pompidou in Paris, and M+ in Hong Kong have announced a new cultural alliance to expand collaboration across exhibitions, public programmes, curatorial research, and conservation. Formalized through an agreement signed on October 30, 2026, the partnership will connect the three institutions while allowing each to retain its independence and identity. Together, the museums serve approximately 10 million in-person visitors annually and reach 125 million people online. Planned initiatives include jointly organized exhibitions, long-term loans, shared research, and expanded access to their collections of modern and contemporary art, creating new opportunities for international exchange across three continents.

Best Hiking Socks: Gift Guide

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

Serious hikers and backpackers have many different socks for various conditions and footwear. You’ll be surprised by the variety, and they’ll be grateful to receive one or more as gifts. In addition to normal socks and dry-weather socks, hikers need waterproof socks, sleeping socks, compression socks to recover from injuries, splinter-resistant socks for cabins…and the list goes on. An army marches on its feet, as the saying goes. Hiking socks are a gift that keeps on giving, and they make great stocking stuffers too! Santa approves.

Darn Tough Socks are the hands-down favorite among serious hikers because they’re so durable. If you do manage to get a hole in one or even wear it thin, the company will replace it for free! Just send it in, and they’ll send you a coupon to order a replacement. I’ve replaced many pairs myself. A hiking and backpacking favorite is the Micro Crew Cushion Sock, available in many colors in men’s and women’s sizes.

REI Darn Tough2. Smartwool Hike Crew SocksWhen new Hikers try Smartwool Hike Light Cushion Crew Socks, they often stick with them for life. That’s brand loyalty! Lightly cushioned, Hike Crew Socks (formerly called PhD Outdoor) are made with Smartwool Indestructawool with extended durability zones. The crew height also adds extra coverage on the trails. These socks are available in a gazillion colors and patterns, so browse away! Sizing is Unisex. REI Smartwool3. Injinji Liner Crew Toe SocksBanish blisters forever with these ingenious Injinji Liner Crew Toe Socks. Wear these light and breathable liner socks under thicker outer socks as a first line of defense against moisture and blisters, or wear them alone as your go-to socks for everyday adventures. Made with Coolmax, these synthetic socks wrap each toe individually, preventing the friction that causes blisters. REI Injinji4. Dachstein Boiled Wool SocksDachstein is the world’s foremost manufacturer of boiled wool clothing (including their famous boiled wool mittens, which also make a great holiday gift.) Boiled wool is very dense, warm, and effectively waterproof because it’s so thick and tightly woven. Made in Austria, these socks are worn for winter hiking, mountaineering, and sleeping in a winter sleeping bag. Amazon5. Brushtail Possum Down SocksBrushtail Possum Down Socks are made from New Zealand brushtail possum fur and New Zealand Merino lamb’s wool, and they’re very thick and warm! They are rugged enough for hiking on cold days and make excellent sleep socks. Brushtail possum hair is hollow, just like polar bear hair, which provides incredible warmth and water resistance. While typical wool socks may stay warm when wet, they take forever to dry. If you get these socks wet, the water shakes right out, and they dry quickly. New Zealand’s possums are unrelated to the opossums found in the United States. For extra warmth, pair these with Possumdown Mittens, which are great for hiking on cold mornings! Zpacks Garage Grown Gear6. KUIU Ultra Merino Over-the-Calf Wool SocksKUIU’s ULTRA Merino socks are designed for high-stress outdoor activity where additional technical support of the feet is required. The style and shape complement a wide range of footwear while adding protection to your feet. Highly breathable and moisture-wicking, this sock molds to your feet, reducing shear friction and maintaining optimal skin integrity. Made with Nuyarn wool, they are stronger than the average Merino wool on the market, yet they boast the same natural odor resistance and comfort. Additional yarn thickness in high-wear areas helps protect the feet with cushioning and adds longevity to the life of the sock. Elastic fibers are knitted through the middle of the sock to maintain a strong grip around the arch and ankle, minimizing friction and blistering. KUIU7. Far Pointe Polartec Alpha Camp SocksPolartec Alpha fleece is an ultralight, gauzy polyester fabric originally developed for the military and widely adopted by the ultralight backpacking community for its incredible warmth-to-weight ratio. Highly breathable and fast-drying, it’s an efficient insulation layer that’s perfect for making camp socks to keep your feet warm in a sleeping bag or under a backpacking quilt. These Far Pointe Camp Socks are for backpackers and campers who suffer from cold feet at night, and at just 25 grams per pair, they’re an easy addition to a backpacking gear list. They’re only recommended for sleeping in or lounging around at home, tucked into your bunny slippers. Garage Grown Gear8. Western Mountaineering Flash Down BootiesThere is nothing better than slipping out of your hiking, mountaineering, or ski boots and into a pair of lightweight Western Mountaineering Men’s Flash Booties at the end of the day. These babies are packed with warm goose down insulation to help thaw out cold feet and give them some good ol’ TLC. The Flash Bootie is primarily intended for use indoors, in your tent, or even in your sleeping bag, but a reinforced sole does allow for quick trip to the head before bed. Backcountry.com Garage Grown Gear9. NRS 0.5 Hydroskin WetSocksNRS HydroSkin WetSocks are the ultimate liner for hiking through snow, streams, or other wet and cold conditions. They are thin enough to easily slip into your boots, trail runners, or sandals. The neoprene core insulates and protects your feet even when wet, and the 4-way-stretch PowerSpan outer layer adds mobility and durability. These socks are a must-have if you insist on wearing trail runners or light hikers when there’s snow on the ground. The added pairing snaps prevent you from hitting the river with only one sock. REI Zpacks10. Darn Tough Mountaineering SocksDarn Tough’s Over-the-Calf Mountaineering Socks are seriously warm. They have a streamlined performance fit (which means they won’t fall down) and are chock-full of merino wool for superior wicking and all-day warmth. Their over-the-calf height and extra cushioning make them ideal for long-distance hiking and climbing through subzero temperatures. Fine-gauge knitting ups the durability factor and offers a “put it on, forget it’s on” feel. Their fabric blend pulls moisture away from the skin and dries quickly for comfort. REI Darn Tough11. CEP Compression SocksThese CEP Compression Socks have many uses. They prevent blisters, significantly reduce swelling, and can accelerate healing by promoting blood flow for lower leg ailments like sprained ankles, plantar fasciitis, or Achilles tendonitis. CEP is the gold standard in sports medicine compression socks. You don’t need these socks very often, but when you get injured, which happens to all serious hikers sooner or later, they’re the key to healing faster. CEP Compression Socks12. Rab Vapor Barrier SocksWinter hikers and mountaineers wear vapor barrier socks in subzero conditions to prevent perspiration from making their socks and boots damp. Your feet sweat 1/2 cup of perspiration every day, and keeping your footwear dry is a significant challenge. These Rab Vapor Barrier Socks are worn next to your feet and then covered with warmer socks. Made from siliconized nylon, they’re completely waterproof, so no moisture can pass through. A curious thing happens to feet that are covered with a non-breathable material in cold conditions – they stop perspiring. It’s magic. Rab13. Sockwell Cadence Compression SocksWith graduated compression and minimal cushioning, Cadence compression socks offer Sockwell’s lightest-weight fit for comfort during prolonged sitting, standing, flying, or exercise. Crafted with fine merino wool and rayon from bamboo, with spandex throughout the socks for a superior fit and comfort, their moderate (15–20 mmHg) graduated compression promotes circulation, minimizes swelling, and reduces fatigue. REI14. WRIGHTSOCK Double Layer Silver Escape Crew SocksMade with patented double-layer construction, WRIGHTSOCK Double Layer Silver Escape socks deliver cushioning and odor resistance for many miles of cushioned comfort on the trail. Their patented double-layer, lightly cushioned design reduces friction, excessive moisture, and heat to minimize hot spots and blisters, while silver extruded into yarn offers natural antimicrobial protection, which inhibits the growth of odor-causing bacteria on the fabric. REI15. Darn Tough Steeley Boot Cushion SocksDelivering fully cushioned comfort from the toe boxes to the cuffs, the men’s Darn Tough Steely Boot Cushion socks are ready to protect your feet around the clock. Performance fit helps eliminate slipping, bunching, and blisters. Boot height sits mid-calf for added protection and warmth. NRS HydroSkin WetSocks are the ultimate liner for hiking through snow, streams, or other wet and cold conditions. They are thin enough to easily slip into your boots, trail runners, or sandals. The neoprene core insulates and protects your feet even when wet, and the 4-way-stretch PowerSpan outer layer adds mobility and durability. These socks are a must-have if you insist on wearing trail runners or light hikers when there’s snow on the ground. The added pairing snaps prevent you from hitting the river with only one sock.

Darn Tough’s Over-the-Calf Mountaineering Socks are seriously warm. They have a streamlined performance fit (which means they won’t fall down) and are chock-full of merino wool for superior wicking and all-day warmth. Their over-the-calf height and extra cushioning make them ideal for long-distance hiking and climbing through subzero temperatures. Fine-gauge knitting ups the durability factor and offers a “put it on, forget it’s on” feel. Their fabric blend pulls moisture away from the skin and dries quickly for comfort.

CEP is the gold standard in sports medicine compression socks. Stephan James Cut Weight in Order to Look Bigger for ‘I Play Rocky’

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

Sign up below to receive our newest workout routines, recipes, news stories, and offers from our partners

With the right plan and the right discipline, you can get seriously shredded in just 28 days.

At age 62, "Big Bill" shares his wisdom to dominate one of the ultimate strength marks.

Follow these fit women we're crushing on for inspiration, workout ideas, and motivation.

Stephan James is no stranger to playing a real-life person on screen. The award-winning actor put his best foot forward as Olympic running legend, Jesse Owens, in Race so he understands the importance of getting in shape for athletic roles. But to play the late, great Carl Weathers in I Play Rocky, the star tells M&F that he actually cut weight in order to look like his far larger hero.

Carl Weathers was in the NFL before his big break in the movies, but once the former Oakland Raider was cast as boxing champ Apollo Creed in the original Rocky, not to mention three successful sequels, it became clear that the star could blend his physicality as an athlete with the charisma required for the big screen.

At 6’2’’ and topping the scales at around 220 muscular pounds, Weathers frame is a difficult one to replicate. “So, that was my first thought: I had to put on a lot of weight, gotta put on a lot of muscle,” says James. “I was just over 200 pounds when I found out that I was going to play him in this film. And, speaking to one of my trainers, I was telling him about this plan to just start packing on all this weight and trying to turn it into muscle. And, he was like, ‘You know what, Steph? If you just try to cut your bodyfat percentage down, then you may give yourself a better chance, giving the perspective of somebody who is even bigger than you may actually be.’”

The tactic was a savvy one. Competitive bodybuilders have long known that decreasing levels of body fat makes for a more muscular looking physique. As fat stores shrink, the skin appears tighter against the muscle, making the most of those angles, edges, and separations that create definition. And, as the waistline decreases, the upper body appears wider, making the shoulders, chest, and arms look wider.

“I was able to cut down to about 180,” shares James, who continued to train for his fighting scenes while on a calorie deficit. “It really helped just emphasize and accentuate, you know, the muscles that I had. So, I was proud of what was the end result in the film.”

The Canadian actor says that he had just two months to get in shape to play Weathers, who passed away in 2024, and was hailed as Sylvester Stallone’s favorite onscreen opponent. James tells M&F that while he was previously “not big on cardio” before gaining the role, he soon understood that the iconic boxing battles between Creed and “The Italian Stallion” would force him to embrace the grind.

“I had to get very big on it during the process of making this film,” explains James, noting that mornings would consist of a cardio and weight training session, followed by learning the craft of boxing in the afternoons. “There was so much that I didn’t know and understand,” he recalls of stepping between the ropes. “From the footwork, to the technical, punches, then starting to learn the choreography. Cardio is the number one thing that you get from boxing. So, I think all of these things helped me cut the weight really fast, helping me to shred really, really, quickly.” The hard work certainly paid off, as Rocky fans have been quick to praise the casting of I Play Rocky, the story of how Sylvester Stallone defied the odds: writing and starring in one of the biggest movies of all time.

While Anthony Ippolito has Sly’s voice and body language down to a fine art, there’s more than a passing resemblance between Stephan James and Carl Weathers. The actor, who alongside brother Shamier Anderson, champions the representation of black talent with their nonprofit, The Black Academy, explains that he has a great deal of respect for the doors that Carl Weathers opened up for future generations. “Guys like Carl helped pave the way,” he tells M&F. “For so many other actors, like myself, to be able to be a leading man, to be seen as layered, characters with heart, thoughts, emotions, charisma and charm. Even his comedic beats. It’s not lost on me what Carl meant for that time, just being a pioneer.”

I Play Rocky hits theaters in the U.S. from Nov. 20.

Tributes honoring the coach and content creator poured in from family and fans.

Bodybuilder AJ Ellison Shares 3 tips for Fat Loss After 50

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

Sign up below to receive our newest workout routines, recipes, news stories, and offers from our partners

With the right plan and the right discipline, you can get seriously shredded in just 28 days.

At age 62, "Big Bill" shares his wisdom to dominate one of the ultimate strength marks.

Follow these fit women we're crushing on for inspiration, workout ideas, and motivation.

IFBB Pro and Masters Men’s Physique Competitor Adam J. Ellison may be 50 years old, but he has a physique that would make most men half his age green with envy. Fortunately, the proud father of three kids says that it is still possible to drop fat while juggling the responsibilities of work and family, as he shared his “No B.S. guide” on Instagram.

AJ Ellison knows that fat mass tends to increase while lean muscle decreases with age, and science suggests that the slow down in metabolic rates could play a significant role as to why it is harder to drop body fat as we age, but that doesn’t mean that we should ditch our physique goals  as we mature. Ellison, now 50 years old, is still winning competitions on stage, and looked phenomenal as he topped the Master’s 50+ Men’s Physique category at the 2026 IFBB Masters World Championships. He even placed second in the 40+, meaning that AJ is still hanging with those who are up to a decade younger than himself. With all that in mind, here are his tips for dropping stubborn fat.

“Fat loss doesn’t start in the gym, it starts in your kitchen,” wrote Ellison. “You can’t out-train a poor diet.” The big man also said that he makes sure to consume plenty of protein. “Aim for 50 to 70g of protein at every meal. Build every plate around protein first. Fill the rest with whole foods.”

“Most men try to burn more calories. That’s backwards,” said Ellison. “Protect your muscle first.” While muscle does not burn calories directly, the presence of more muscle tissue means that there is a greater level of energy required in the body, leading to a greater calorie requirement.

To build muscle, the competitive bodybuilder suggested that we “Lift 3 to 4 times every week,” and added that for hypertrophy, “Progress your lifts whenever possible.”

“Cardio is a tool. It isn’t the foundation,” believes Ellison, who utilizes cardiovascular training to support his efforts without making it the center of his ethos. “Strength training should always come first,” wrote the master, for his almost 900K Instagram followers.

To make sure that cardio is part of the mix, “Add 8,000 to 10,000 daily steps,” enthused Ellison. “Use cardio to support recovery, not to punish yourself.”

Summing up, the bodybuilder noted that “Small calorie deficits beat aggressive diets,” observing that “Crash dieting teaches your body to conserve energy.” So, to get started, Ellison coached that we should aim to lose 0.5 to 1% of body fat per week, and to trust in the process. “Track your progress for at least three weeks before changing anything,” he concluded.

The new division has already created new stars, and she’s excited to be a pioneer of it.

Here’s why the IFBB Pro calls these moves “character builders.”

Marketers Are Writing A New Playbook For Women’s Sports

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

For decades, women’s sports have been considered an afterthought.

Women’s sports fans had to “go chasing” to find a platform or channel that streamed the games, said Karina LeBlanc, EVP of strategic growth and development at RAJ Sports, during a panel at Treasure AI’s Agentic World conference in Miami.

As a former professional soccer player, LeBlanc saw how the inaccessibility of women’s sports frustrated fans and players alike.

These days, she said, women’s sports are easier to access and reach a much wider audience, but the work still isn’t done. So RAJ Sports, a sports operator and investor, elected to partner with Treasure AI, an agentic CDP and marketing platform, to gain a better understanding of who its customers are and what they want out of women’s sports.

“For 85% of my career,” LeBlanc said, “the marketing strategy around women’s sports was essentially, ‘Here’s what the men did. Let’s do it for women.’”

But women’s sports is a whole different ball game. (No pun intended.)

RAJ Sports, which owns the WNBA team the Portland Thorns and the NWSL team the Portland Fire, was looking to flip the tired women’s sports playbook.

In addition to RAJ’s own first-party data, which includes ticket and merchandise sales as well as social media and email engagement, it receives leaguewide data from the WNBA and the NWSL, all of which is funneled into its data warehouse.

But having data isn’t the same as being able to make use of it and understand how to reach new customers. The biggest challenge for RAJ Sports has been “making sense of the whole pool of fans” across both teams, John Torris, chief commercial officer at RAJ Sports, told AdExchanger.

Treasure AI has helped them develop audience segments and determine who to target with what content, said Torris.

In men’s sports, a new fan’s first interaction with a team tends to be buying a ticket to a game, said Torris.

But for women’s sports, the data revealed that the entry point for many fans is buying merch. By the time they choose to attend a game, he added, “they feel like they have a connection with the club or the players.”

This, of course, means that marketing to potential new fans looks entirely different for men’s versus women’s sports. Last year, RAJ Sports opted to take the Fire’s ecommerce and merchandising strategy in-house, said Torris. The move helped RAJ influence the fan experience directly by developing what Torris called “expressive” merchandise and apparel that helps cultivate an “intimate relationship” with the team. The Thorns are currently in the process of taking their ecommerce in-house for next year’s season.

One piece of apparel sold at the Fire’s “Sneakerheads Night” was a T-shirt featuring pictures of Nike sneakers designed by the team’s players. The shirts sold out in minutes of the arena opening.

But T-shirt sales alone aren’t enough to keep a business afloat.

RAJ Sports also deploys ads through a blend of social media, email and linear TV to get more loyal viewers and ticket buyers.

The company can run many of its commercials through its own sports network, Rose City SportsNet. The network is a local station that streams all Portland Fire matches, as well as a number of Thorns games.

And, of course, in addition to the linear option, RAJ Sports also has a paid streaming app, called Portland Fire+, where fans can access live programming and behind-the-scenes content on mobile.

As women’s sports continue to expand, more brands are pursuing partnerships with WNBA and NWSL teams.

Partnering with a sports team can serve as a “validator” for brands, particularly emerging ones, according to Torris. Sports fans are often “loyal to a fault,” he quipped, meaning that brand partnerships can rapidly build exposure and relationships with a team’s fanbase – as long as the brand resonates with the fans’ values.

For instance, both the Thorns and Fire have a partnership with baby product brand Nuna. The partnership includes activations aimed at mothers and their children, like on-field toddler and baby races during halftime.

While men’s sports also have a strong sense of “fandom and loyalty,” the energy around women’s sports – and thus the messaging that resonates with them – is entirely different, said Torris. Men’s sports fandom tends to be more focused on wins and losses and the quality of the athletes’ performance, he said, whereas women’s sports fans tend to be more enthusiastic about the sense of community and the broader experience of being at the game. Though, of course, they still want their own team to win.

If the Portland Fire loses a game, “we would have a standing ovation with 15,000 plus people cheering them on through the end,” said Torris. “Nobody leaves, and I haven’t seen that in men’s sports.”

Rather than encouraging people to come watch the local team win, RAJ Sports instead focuses its advertising on the experience itself. “The message is like, ‘Come and try it, and we hope you come back,’” Torris said.

As LeBlanc put it, based on her own experience, “People are like, ‘What is this energy?’ Because it’s inspired and it’s positive. And people are like, ‘I want more.’”

AI is taking over the media and tech industries in the name of simplification, and marketers are pining for provable campaign outcomes, including from TV. These shifts are also causing significant change in Agency Holdco Land, according to Angela Steele, the first chief client officer for WPP Media US.

No major retail media player has taken as circumspect an approach to ad revenue as Shopify. But the company has recently taken its next important step with a product called Campaign Autopilot.

OpenAI’s head of monetization, Benji Shomair makes the case for ads as a way to bring ChatGPT to more people, with user trust taking priority over advertiser demands.

In Q3, The Trade Desk beat the admittedly pessimistic revenue forecast it set for itself earlier this year. But growth has stalled.

Viant is doubling down on CTV with its $20 million Waymark acquisition, betting that AI-generated ads can bring more advertisers to streaming TV.

TIME is using AI agents to draft proposals and troubleshoot campaigns – but not to replace the human relationships that drive ad sales.

AdExchanger is where marketers, agencies, publishers and tech companies go for the latest information on the trends that are transforming digital media and marketing, from data, privacy, identity and AI to commerce, CTV, measurement and mobile.

→ Baca artikel asli

AI Shopping Errors: What To Fix To Help Shoppers Choose Your Products

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

Identify practical ways to improve location visibility in AI-generated answers and recommendations.

This rundown shows how to test briefs, audits, or rank reporting with a four-layer system built on your own data.

Spend less time on publishing and reports, and more on helpful content. November 18, 2 PM ET.

Invalid traffic on AI Max search doubled to 5.28%. Get the channel-by-channel retail benchmarks before Black Friday.

Ask the SEJ experts you already read what AI, agents, and algorithm changes mean for your traffic. Private, human to human. $97/mo.

Explore the future of retail with AI shopping. Understand how AI affects product visibility and decision-making for consumers.

This post was sponsored by Writesonic. The opinions expressed in this article are the sponsor’s own.

When shoppers ask ChatGPT or Google what to buy, answers often come back as product cards with prices, ratings, and “buy now” links. AI writes those cards from data spread across the web, but few brands know how those cards position their products.

That’s a risky blind spot to have heading into the holidays. Adobe forecasts AI traffic to US retail sites will grow 130% this holiday season.

AI shopping results usually go wrong in three ways. Your product doesn’t show up, it shows up with the wrong details, or a competitor or reseller gets the final click instead of you. The good news is that these errors trace back to product data you control.

This guide covers what these results look like, why they go wrong, and how to audit your own products.

AI shopping results are product recommendations that appear inside an AI answer. They’re usually triggered by a specific buying question, like “waterproof hiking boots for wide feet under $150.”

In March 2026, OpenAI expanded its Agentic Commerce Protocol (ACP) to cover product discovery. Merchants now share product feeds through ACP so their catalogs appear in ChatGPT.

Retailers like Target, Best Buy, and Wayfair are already integrated. Shopify merchants flow in through Shopify Catalog.

OpenAI dropped in-chat Instant Checkout in March 2026 and now lets merchants run checkout on their own sites. ChatGPT helps you get the shopper, but your product page still has to close the sale.

AI Overviews now show product cards for many shopping searches, drawing on Google’s Shopping Graph. Merchants add their products mainly by uploading feeds to Google Merchant Center. Google says the Shopping Graph holds over 50 billion product listings.

AI Mode and Gemini use the same Shopping Graph, and Google is rolling out agentic checkout there too. In this scenario, Google’s agent buys a tracked item with Google Pay once the shopper confirms. To qualify, merchants need a Merchant Center account and a Universal Commerce Protocol (UCP) integration.

The most common AI shopping issues fall into three groups.

Most brands find these issues by accident. Someone runs a prompt, sees a wrong price, and fixes that one product. Hundreds of other prompts go unchecked.

The common thread is consistency. When your feed, product page, and retailer listings disagree on price, stock, or naming, AI has to pick one. It doesn’t always pick right.

An analysis reported by Search Engine Journal tracked ChatGPT product picks that came from integrated feeds. In one day in July 2026, their share went from 8.26% to 61.54%. Of 517 tracked stores with visibility swings of 33% or more, 450 saw drops.

Merchant variety shrank too. The top 10 merchants’ share of references grew from 22.5% to 41.8%. Stores without a connected feed now risk losing picks they used to win.

Your product also lives on Amazon, Walmart, Target, and other retailers. Each listing can show a different price, title, or stock status. AI may link to whichever one looks most complete.

Product schema, plus identifiers like Global Trade Item Number (GTIN), manufacturer part number (MPN), brand, and variant, tells AI exactly which item it’s looking at. Without them, AI can mix up models, sizes, and colors.

AI leans hard on what third-party websites say about your brand and products. Writesonic’s June 2026 study of 2,000+ brands found YouTube or Reddit mentions made a brand about three times more likely to show up in AI answers. Mentions on its own site raised that just 1.3 times.

Manual audits break down fast. Each check is only a snapshot, and AI answers change constantly.

Results vary by platform, region, and day, so rerun the same prompts over time. Daily checks give you the most accurate picture. Audit at the category level too, because a brand can dominate headphones and be invisible in speakers. How do you do this? A strong AI Visibility platform will help you collect, understand and action this data.

Writesonic’s study of 631,999 prompt runs across seven AI platforms found the top-ranked brand in ChatGPT rotated on 52% of prompts when the same prompt was rerun.

Writesonic’s AI Shopping Tracker automates those checks daily across ChatGPT and Google AI Overviews. It also shows how often your prompts trigger shopping results at all, which manual audits miss.

It maps directly to the problems covered above:

Any tracker only sees the prompts you give it, so the prompt set from your audit is the foundation. Start with your best sellers and the categories you can’t afford to lose this season.

The brands that check what AI says about their products, and fix the data behind it, will own these product cards. Everyone else is letting a model decide how their products are shown.

Les Balcons de Sermenaz / HEMAA Architectes

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

Text description provided by the architects. Located along the ridge between the Rhône and Saône rivers, Rillieux-la-Pape occupies a unique position within the metropolitan area. The town combines an historic village centre with numerous large residential developments that open out to the east onto an extensive protected natural area stretching along the hillside. It is at the edge of this landscape that the Les Balcons de Sermenaz residential development takes shape. As the final component of a new neighborhood, it forms a link with the park and accompanies the gradual transition from the urban fabric to the natural environment. This unusual setting oﬀers the opportunity to build in direct relationship with a largely preserved landscape.

The town combines an historic village centre with numerous large residential developments that open out to the east onto an extensive protected natural area stretching along the hillside. It is at the edge of this landscape that the Les Balcons de Sermenaz residential development takes shape. As the final component of a new neighborhood, it forms a link with the park and accompanies the gradual transition from the urban fabric to the natural environment. This unusual setting oﬀers the opportunity to build in direct relationship with a largely preserved landscape.

Frankie Muniz Breaks His Silence on Being Hospitalized After Racing Crash

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

UPDATE — 11/12/26, 12:40 a.m. ET: Malcolm in the Middle alum Frankie Muniz is assuring worried fans he is on the mend after being hospitalized in Florida.

A Wednesday, November 11, statement from Ford Racing confirmed that Muniz, 40, had been released from the hospital after several hours under observation. The actor turned racing driver was injured in a crash that occurred on a practice lap at the Daytona International Speedway prior to the Proud to Honor Veterans Day Classic event.

“I am disappointed I won’t be able to take part in tonight’s Ford’s Proud to Honor event tonight,” Muniz said in an accompanying statement. “I was looking forward to honoring the service of our veterans and racing against these other amazing drivers.”

Muniz offered an update on his physical condition, admitting he was “a little sore” after being rushed off the racetrack to receive medical attention hours earlier.

“My Mustang did its job in protecting me,” he stressed. “I want to thank everyone at Ford Racing, the Daytona International Speedway and the staff here at the hospital.”

The star added, “I especially wanted to thank everyone who has reached out and for all the prayers.”

Frankie Muniz was hospitalized after being involved in a crash at the Daytona International Speedway.

Us has learned that Muniz crashed on Wednesday, November 11 during a practice lap ahead of Ford’s Proud to Honor Veterans Day Classic.

“Frankie Muniz was involved in an incident while practicing for today’s Proud to Honor event at Daytona International Speedway,” a Ford spokesperson told Us. “He was transported to a local medical facility for further evaluation. He is awake and alert.”

Us has also reached out to Muniz’s team for comment. Entertainment Tonight was first to report the news.

Last month, the Malcolm in the Middle actor sat down with Us and shared how excited he was to participate in an event aimed to honor all six branches of the Armed Services. (Muniz’s grandfather, John Preli, served in the U.S. Navy during World War II, while his father, Francisco, served in the Navy and Air Force for over 20 years as a mechanic.)

“We all owe so much to live in this amazing country to the military,” Muniz shared in October. “It’s going to be an ultra-competitive race because we’re race car drivers, and that’s how we roll. But it’s going to be a blast, and I’m thrilled to be a part of it.”

Although the Ford driver grew up acting in projects like Agent Cody Banks and Big Fat Liar, Muniz has since turned his focus to racing.

When comparing the sport to acting, Muniz is grateful racing is not subjective.

“It’s not someone’s opinion whether you’re the best or you’re the fastest,” he explained. “I like — not to toot my own horn but — there are only 100 drivers on the planet that have a NASCAR license right now, and I’m one of them, and I’m so grateful for that, and I want to take advantage of the opportunity and do all I can to succeed as much as I possibly can in this world because I know I’m lucky and I truly truly love it.”

Muniz continued, “The thought of stopping racing scares the heck out of me because I do get so much joy and happiness. … I loved what I got to do as an actor 100 percent, especially lately. … but nothing quite compares to that feeling of being fastest or winning or having a good race or something crazy happened. I think that that’s something that you just — I can’t compare it to anything else I’ve ever done, and that’s why I want to continue doing it as long as I can, while I can, for sure.”

When your Body’s Operating System Glitches

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

The autonomic nervous system regulates everything from heart rate to digestion. Abnormalities may contribute to ADHD and POTS symptoms.

You probably don’t give much thought to how your heart speeds up during exercise or how blood continues flowing to your brain when you stand up. These essential functions are managed by the autonomic nervous system, the body’s complex operating system, which runs quietly in the background.

The autonomic nervous system controls heart rate, blood pressure, the digestive process, body temperature, and continuous blood circulation in the presence of gravity — squeezing blood out of the legs and torso and redistributing it to the head and heart when you move from lying down or sitting to standing.

Some studies suggest that autonomic nervous system abnormalities may contribute to ADHD symptoms like inattention and impulsiveness. Clinicians may notice and use these symptoms to diagnose ADHD, but the underlying cause might reflect atypical autonomic nervous system activity.

Postural Orthostatic Tachycardia Syndrome (POTS) is an atypical or malfunctioning state of the autonomic nervous system. People with ADHD and/or hypermobility appear to have a higher prevalence of POTS than do members of the general population. Symptoms include dizziness or fainting when standing up from a sitting or lying position due to sudden changes in blood pressure and heart rate.

[Read: ADHD’s Curious Connections to Chronic Inflammation and Dysautonomia]

In individuals with hypermobility, autonomic dysregulation may be caused by irritation of the nerves that are intermittently stretched due to lax ligaments at the craniocervical junction, where the brain and upper spine meet.

A tilt table test, which measures your heart rate and blood pressure as you change position, is the main way POTS is diagnosed. Other assessments include blood and urine tests and a measure of the autonomic nerves that control sweating.

[Read: Exploring the ADHD-Inflammation Link]

Vagus and sympathetic nerve stimulation systems can help by:

Arthur L. Jenkins, III, M.D., FACS, FAANS, is a board-certified neurosurgeon and founder of Jenkins NeuroSpine in New York and Connecticut.

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

SpaceX launched a classified mission for the U.S. Space Force from California tonight (Nov. 11), the second such flight for the company in a six-day span.

A Falcon 9 rocket lifted off from California's Vandenberg Space Force Base tonight at 11:36 p.m. EST (8:36 p.m. local California time; 0136 GMT on Nov. 12), kicking off a mission called USSF-627.

Vandenberg hosted a similar liftoff less than a week ago: On Nov. 6, a Falcon 9 sent the USSF-432 mission skyward from the site, which lies on California's gorgeous but often foggy central coast.

We don't know much about USSF-627 or USSF-432; both are classified. Some experts have speculated, however, that each mission launched a batch of SpaceX's Starshield satellites for the Space Force's Space Based Sensing and Targeting portfolio, which helps the military branch track potential targets around the world.

SpaceX didn't reveal details about either USSF-627 or USSF-432, and the company ended the webcast of both launches shortly after the first stages of their Falcon 9 rockets came back to Earth.

In both cases, that touchdown occurred about 8.5 minutes after liftoff on the SpaceX droneship "Of Course I Still Love You," which was stationed downrange of Vandenberg in the Pacific Ocean.

It was the seventh mission for USSF-627's Falcon 9 booster, which carries the designation 1103.

NROL-179 | SDA-T1TL-E | SDA-T1TL-A | 3 Starlink missions

USSF-627 was the 120th Falcon 9 launch of 2026 and the rocket's 15th national-security flight of the year to date. Eighty-five of the 120 flights have been devoted to building out SpaceX's Starlink broadband constellation in low Earth orbit, which currently consists of more than 11,000 satellites.

The Falcon 9 is not on a record-breaking cadence this year. In 2025, the rocket flew a whopping 165 missions, and 122 of them were Starlink flights.

The Super Bowl’s Prediction Market Ad Ban Stands; Retailers Aren’t Open To AI

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

The midterm elections were a marketing moment tailor-made for prediction markets Kalshi and Polymarket. But the next mass attention moment for American media – the Super Bowl – won’t be a friendly environment for these platforms, Adweek reports.

Although the NFL, like most pro sports leagues, enthusiastically embraced sports betting partnerships and marketing, it’s nevertheless sticking with the prediction market ad ban it put in place for last season’s Super Bowl.

The NFL even clarified ahead of this season that its sportsbook marketing partners DraftKings, FanDuel and Fanatics can’t promote their own prediction market offerings during NFL games.

Those same sportsbooks launched Kalshi and Polymarket alternatives this year as competition heated up between unabashed gambling apps and “we swear we’re not gambling” prediction markets. These gambling apps now default to prediction market mode in states that prohibit sports betting. It’s a cheeky move. Gambling apps are now using the same argument as prediction markets to sidestep gambling regulations.

Given the NFL’s ad ban, prediction markets will therefore have to get creative with their Super Bowl marketing strategy.

They won’t be able to partner with NFL players or teams like they have in other leagues, because the NFL kiboshed those deals, too. But that doesn’t rule out deals with creators and celebrities, news publisher partnerships or live activations in the greater Los Angeles area, where this season’s Big Game will take place.

Many marketers and entrepreneurs insist that agentic shopping is the future.

And there are already signs that consumers are curious. Just look at Meta’s new personal agent, Muse. Between its Sept. 8 launch and Meta’s third quarter earnings call on Oct. 21, more than 10 million people in the US and Canada set up their own Muse agent and roughly 25% of them now use it on any given day, according to CEO Mark Zuckerberg.

But in order for agentic shopping to really take off, retailers need to be on board – and, so far, the response has been mixed.

Amazon, for instance, has been blocking Muse since shortly after it launched, in part to keep shoppers within its own recommendation and discovery channels. Having an agent do your shopping is bad for Amazon’s ad revenue.

Then, earlier this week, United Airlines announced that AI bots will be prohibited from booking flights, citing data privacy concerns as well as potential violations of Federal Aviation Administration and Transportation Department rules.

As The Wall Street Journal puts it, “An AI agent can’t, for example, attest during check-in that bags don’t contain explosives.”

YouTube gives marketers plenty of content to choose from – and plenty of reasons to second-guess their choices.

Should they go for high-production-value video from professional broadcasters and studios? Low-rent, niche creator content? Or highly produced content from well-resourced, big-name creator channels?

Naturally, there are merits to all three, Mike Shields reports at Next in Media.

Creator channels – which run the gamut from MrBeast to the guy streaming his playthrough of the video game you just bought to a few hundred users – tend to have the widest reach on YouTube, accounting for 63% of all viewing sessions, according to TV attention measurement platform TVision. Professional channels, like Apple TV and Comedy Central’s The Daily Show, account for the rest.

But TVision found that the professional content drives 34% deeper engagement than the creator stuff.

However, in a weird inversion of that trend, professionally produced large creator channels drive lower engagement than niche channels that only attract between 1,000 and 13,000 viewers.

Marketers could use such third-party insights to curate their own YouTube inventory, Shields notes. But targeting specific creator channels via Google Ads is a convoluted process involving manually pasting in approved channels.

Don’t worry, though. There will be an AI agent for that soon enough.

UK broadcaster ITV saw a nearly 8% decline in advertising revenue last quarter and expects a similar 5% to 10% decline in the fourth quarter. [The Hollywood Reporter]

How Kickstarter’s Everette Taylor went from CMO to CEO. [WSJ]

Why are streaming services still trying to become TikTok? [The Verge]

New York or Nowhere says it built its apparel brand on organic social posts and collaborations – without spending a dime on ads. [Business Insider]

AI is taking over the media and tech industries in the name of simplification, and marketers are pining for provable campaign outcomes, including from TV. These shifts are also causing significant change in Agency Holdco Land, according to Angela Steele, the first chief client officer for WPP Media US.

No major retail media player has taken as circumspect an approach to ad revenue as Shopify. But the company has recently taken its next important step with a product called Campaign Autopilot.

OpenAI’s head of monetization, Benji Shomair makes the case for ads as a way to bring ChatGPT to more people, with user trust taking priority over advertiser demands.

In Q3, The Trade Desk beat the admittedly pessimistic revenue forecast it set for itself earlier this year. But growth has stalled.

Viant is doubling down on CTV with its $20 million Waymark acquisition, betting that AI-generated ads can bring more advertisers to streaming TV.

TIME is using AI agents to draft proposals and troubleshoot campaigns – but not to replace the human relationships that drive ad sales.

She Is Rewriting Mexico’s Ballads, One Woman’s Story at a Time

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

Grammy-nominated songwriter Vivir Quintana is known throughout Latin America for giving voice to women who fought back against domestic violence.

Grammy-nominated songwriter Vivir Quintana is known throughout Latin America for giving voice to women who fought back against domestic violence.

Against the backdrop of a centuries-old stone church in the Mexican city of Oaxaca, the singer Vivir Quintana raised her deep, husky voice above the blare of trumpets, trombones and the booming pulse of a tuba.

Behind her stood a brass band of young Indigenous girls in embroidered white blouses and brightly colored skirts. Hat in hand and fist raised, Ms. Quintana sang a corrido, a traditional Mexican folk ballad usually used to recount tales of revolutionaries, outlaws, folk heroes, and more recently, drug traffickers.

Corridos are typically sung by men, about men. Ms. Quintana sang a corrido she wrote based on the true story of a woman who fought back when her abusive partner tried to gouge out her eyes with a knife.

“I used to think that what united us women was our pain. Now I have realized it is love and resistance,” Ms. Quintana told the crowd in October during a women’s conference in Oaxaca called Decididas, drawing a roar of applause. “We must keep defending ourselves!”

On Thursday, Ms. Quintana will take the stage at the Latin Grammy Awards in Las Vegas alongside the Colombian pop superstar Juanes, with whom she is nominated for song of the year for “Humano.” It is her second Latin Grammy nomination.

TwitchCon is at a crossroads as creators question its value

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

Join us Dec. 1-3 in Nashville for the Digiday Programmatic Marketing Summit

TwitchCon remains a draw for brands and streaming companies, but some creators say the cost, safety concerns and uncertain payoff have made the annual convention harder to justify.

Ahead of TwitchCon 2026’s kick off this weekend in San Diego, allegations spread on X that Twitch was giving away tickets, and that ticket counts for Twitch Partners, the top-performing, monetized creators, were the lowest they’d been in years. Though Twitch wouldn’t outright say attendance was down, a spokesperson told Digiday that “events have changed a lot in the past few years,” after the post-COVID “crazy high demand.” They confirmed the company was giving away tickets through their Ambassador program, which it has done in previous years.

Digiday spoke with six creators and two brands showing up at the convention about what this might signal for the streaming platform’s biggest annual event and the state of the streaming creator economy.

“I don’t feel like we have a clear enough understanding of what TwitchCon is today and how I could add value to its programming by being there,” said Justin Miclat, chief growth officer at talent and brand management company Click Media. Miclat also founded the Kinetic Group talent agency, which has worked with live streamers. Last year, the group spent $50,000 producing a TwitchCon event promoting and showcasing women in the industry.

TwitchCon, the IRL event for the livestream platform Twitch, which accrued 900 million hours of watch time last year, has historically been a place where creators connect with fans, sponsors and their peers. Past and present sponsors include State Farm, e.l.f. Cosmetics, Streamlabs, Southwest Airlines, Honda, and the platform’s parent company, Amazon Prime. Creators often spend the weekend seeking out new partnerships opportunities.

Recently, however, TwitchCon (which is typically held in California every year along with a European counterpart) has faced scrutiny from attendees around security issues, rising costs, less opportunities and a lack of clarity around what the platform considers its core community. Top creator Emily-Beth “Emiru” Schunk was assaulted during a meet-and-greet at TwitchCon 2025, prompting backlash and calls for tighter security (and a statement from Twitch). Others pointed to a rash of IRL streamers’ bad behavior while livestreaming at or around the San Diego Convention Center.

Mike Murphy O’Reilly, chief revenue officer at Dexerto, a gaming publisher that is also a full-service creative and marketing agency, said TwitchCon is still a “core event” for the company. “It’s one of the few events across the year where you have a mix of both endemic and non-endemic clients,” he said. Still, he noted that this year’s event “does feel quieter.”

For companies like Streamlabs, which offers a streaming platform regularly used by Twitch creators, the convention is still a must-attend and a must-sponsor. The company sets up content creation stations in the convention center, and creates TwitchCon-themed overlays for livestreams.

“At TwitchCon the percentage of attendees that are actually streamers is generally around 50 percent,” said Ashray Urs, head of Streamlabs. “For us, making broadcast software, that’s a great place to be.” Urs said the convention’s value lies in having so many streamers in one place to get consumer feedback and build new partnerships, as well as meeting key existing brand partners like NVIDIA.

“It’s a perfect endemic fit for us,” Urs said.

But is TwitchCon the perfect fit for creators?

Streamer Tevin “YourBudTevin” Lolar, who has over 50,000 followers on Twitch, told Digiday that creators attending the convention usually fall into one of three categories: low-level streamers looking to learn more and make connections, mid-level streamers hoping to connect with brands, and top streamers, who host events and establish “big money partnerships and deliverables.”

TwitchCon began as an IRL event focused on leveraging its tentpole creators, But as major stars like Kai Cenat and Darren Jason “IShowSpeed” Watkins Jr. have moved beyond the need for the convention, Twitch may have to redefine how it serves creators and fans.

“Twitch needs to hone in on the community-driven streamers and bring them up in order to maintain the health of the convention,” Lolar said. The Twitch spokesperson said the platform wants TwitchCon to reflect the growing community of streamers, while ensuring “gaming is still the heart” of the event.

For mid-tier creators like Veronika “Nikatine” Ripley, a Twitch Ambassador (an initiative that recognizes community leaders on the platform) with over 30,000 followers, TwitchCon isn’t a viable expense anymore. She attended the convention from 2018 through 2023, and was even featured in 2022 marketing materials.

“Do I stand to make more money attending a convention through business deals or meeting potential product vendors or catching up with sponsors? Or do I make more money just streaming instead? In the case of TwitchCon, unfortunately the latter has been true for some time,” she said.

Amira “Xmiramira” Virgil, feels similarly. She was in the first class of Twitch Ambassadors and attended TwitchCon in 2018 and 2019, but the ROI isn’t worth it for her anymore. “The brands I want to work with can be found at conventions and events that don’t cost me upwards of $3,000, and even if they do, there’s still a better ROI,” she said. “I’ve also heard some critique about what brands are attending as well.”

Frogan, a streamer with nearly 70,000 followers who prefers to go by her social media handle, said the event is “way too expensive” and that there are very few incentives for partnered creators.

Quin “CtrlAltQuin” Martin, who has over 10,000 followers on the platform, said stressed the cost. “Traveling to the other side of the country to create free content for Twitch just doesn’t make sense to me at this moment – though I love the platform,” they said.

Nearly everyone Digiday spoke with raised safety concerns. Urs said past TwitchCons felt like there were cameras on you at all times, noting the high volume of IRL creators streaming on the convention floor (this year, Twitch introduced lanyards that signal attendees’ comfort level with being on camera). O’Reilly mentioned the Emiru incident as a detractor for potential attendees.

“Accessibility can sometimes be an afterthought for events inside and outside the ‘con,” said Martin, who uses a mobility device. “Pair that with minimal security and folks who don’t understand boundaries, it can be a hot mess.”

Frogan attended her first TwitchCon in 2022, and said she requested security for 2023’s event after allegedly receiving death threats for her vocal support of Palestine. In 2024, she and several other streamers were temporarily banned from the platform after a Twitch-approved panel held on-stage during TwitchCon drew online backlash and accusations of antisemitism, which she vehemently denies.

“I don’t feel safe going to the event from both physical interactions and Twitch as a company in general,” Frogan said. “I feel like they have failed me as a creator multiple times so it’s not worth it for my peace of mind.”

“We’ve taken the concerns raised following last year’s TwitchCon very seriously,” said the Twitch spokesperson, adding ahead of this weekend’s event, the company has increased investment in security, added more trained safety, and strengthened escalation procedures for on-site teams.

“Our goal is to create an environment where everyone attending TwitchCon feels safe, heard and excited to be there, and we’ll continue listening to our community and making improvements.”

Yahoo is the first licensee of the Internet Licensing Collective, offering publishers a new way to get paid for content used in AI search.

The IAB’s new AI attribution framework promises to standardize measurement, but a lack of platform data leaves much of AI’s impact invisible.

Zach “Mystic Zach” Hirsch has figured out how to maximize engagement around sports-betting content while staying brand safe.

Why brands like Anthropologie are commissioning creator-created shows instead of posts

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

Join us Dec. 1-3 in Nashville for the Digiday Programmatic Marketing Summit

Pooja Tripathi created Brooklyn Coffee Shop, the comedy web series built around a barista with an opinion about every customer who walks through the door. The show won a Webby this year and its fan club in marketing circles keeps growing.

SharkNinja paid to work its Auto Barista machine into an episode, Anthropologie went a step further and hired Tripathi to write and lead its first micro comedy series — a holiday party caper she took from script to shoot. Even instagram has noticed. Tessa Lyons, its vp of product singled out the show as one her favorites while talking up the app’s renewed push into longer-form content.

All of which has turned Tripathi into a social showrunner of sorts, operating in a corner of the creator economy where more brands are starting to shop.

Take Anthropologie – the brand’s marketers were already fans of Tripathi’s work before they got in touch earlier this year.

“We engaged her because we’re fans of her work,” said Barbra Sainsurin, global CEO at Anthropologie Group. The admiration turned out to be mutual and Tripathi “leaped at the chance to create a series for us,” she said. Her comic voice was the draw for the brand, which wanted to try its hand at comedy even though “humour and entertainment haven’t been voices or characters that we’ve incorporated in our story telling traditionally,” said Sainsurin.

The result was the brand’s first micro comedy series “The Holiday Party,” a twist on the seasonal misadventure trope that sits inside its “On My Way” seasonal campaign, which went live last month. Each episode is 90 seconds and will air on the brand’s social channels between November 11–19 alongside behind the scenes footage.

Tripathi developed the characters and the direction of the show while Anthropologie’s team took charge of the styling, from what each character wears to how the party itself was decorated. The goal was a setting that felt true to Tripathi’s audience and also looked like “a party that an Anthro person would throw” said Sainsurin.

The brand took a few more pages from the entertainment playbook for the rollout. Anthropologie is “treating it like a movie launch,” with trailers, teasers and hooks at the end of each episode to pull viewers into the next. Instagram and TikTok are getting the series as are YouTube, Pinterest and Reddit, which means it will be “distributed more broadly than it has ever,” said Sainsurin.

Whether it goes further depends on the numbers. Sainsurin’s team is watching earned reach, how many non-followers the series lands in front of and “how far is this content travelling in the algorithm”. Saves, shares, comments, site visits, product page views and sales will come into the picture once the full spread of holiday content has run. That’s a big shift for a team that doesn’t even do much with creators now. They make up a smaller share of the media mix than at other brands, said Sainsurin.

Putting a price on that kind of work is its own puzzle. Hyphen, the Reign Maker Group-owned talent management firm that brokers series deals, splits the bill into the creator’s fee, production, usage rights, exclusivity and the number of episodes. On its own roster, a single sponsored post from a mid-tier lifestyle creator starts at around $8,000 to $10,000. A multi episode series lands “well into five figures,” and paid usage plus category exclusivity can push the total to six, said Victoria Bachan, CEO of Hyphen. Her team tells brands to weigh that against “ what it would cost to build that audience habit themselves,” she continued.

More brands are starting to have that conversation. They’re hiring social show runners like Tripathi, sometimes in place of part of their influencer marketing spend and sometimes alongside it. In doing so they’ve reopened one of the industry’s longest-running arguments: whether influencer marketing and creator marketing are different things at all.

Both pay someone with an audience to put a product in front of it but they arguably split from there. Influencer marketing leans on the person, whose recommendation and follower count do the heavy lifting on a post that lives and dies on their own account. Creator marketing, on the other hand, heads off toward the content itself. Think characters that viewers get attached to and a format they come back to week after week. All of that sits with the show itself so it holds value well after the influencer post has scrolled out of view.

Take the show We Are MICE, a mockumentary set at a fictional live events agency whose name stands for meetings, incentives, conferences and exhibitions. Beauty creator Genevieve Turley, in her first acting role, plays Maggie, who manages the agency’s team. She’s a 40-something parent who is nearly divorced and recently back at work, trying to pull off flawless events with a team that’s some way short of flawless itself. The clients are real as in they’re the brands that sponsor the series and the plot follows Maggie’s team as it tries to deliver an event for them. Laura Geller Beauty is the first, commissioning six episodes between 60 and 90 seconds that started rolling out across Instagram and TikTok last month (October 26).

The format comes from Rooscreen, the Royal Television Society-nominated production company behind comedy short film Mourning Glory, and was developed with brand partnerships strategists DASH Talent. Like plenty of sitcoms before it, MICE started life in a writer’s room, built around an idea Rooscreen founder Becky Rooney had been carrying around for a while. The characters and their world came first, in other words, and the brands were invited in after.

“Yes, it’s an advert and yes it’s a commercial but rather than a typical product placement or doing a standard influencer post we’ve tried to get into the storyline with the brand,” said Rooney.

“I wanted it to be funny and edgy but also brand safe,” she added.

The plan is for each new series to bring in a different brand and a different event while Maggie and her colleagues, who are also creators, carry their storylines from one to the next. Which leaves room for a different member of the cast to step forward each time, depending on whose audience best fits the brand. A sportswear deal, for instance, would hand the spotlight to Austyn Farrell, a creator and keen marathon runner who plays Mo on the show. “These creators understand their audiences so who better to guide how we advertise certain brands than the people who actually really understand that product,” said Rooney.

Early numbers appear to back that up, or at least as far as views and interactions can tell. As of earlier this week, the episodes had collectively hit 1.3 million views on Instagram with 11,000 interactions.

“Going viral is one thing but if that means reaching two million people who don’t want to buy the product then does that have as much value as reaching 20,000 people who actually do?,” said Rooney.

None of which means brands are turning their backs on creator scale. Anthropologie, for instance, has around 50 creators working on the same campaign. Some are returning partners. Others came through an agency to reach audiences the brand isn’t getting to yet. Similarly SharkNinja works with Tripathi as well as Alex Berry, John Connor Hammon and Ari Caga — the three creators of The ick vertical digital web series — while continuing to work with large groups of creators.

Long gone are the days when LinkedIn was just a job-searching site.

Bitcoin’s daily volume falls to 126K BTC, lowest in 10 years: Will BTC plummet to $60K?

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Amid ongoing volatility, can Bitcoin bulls prevent a pullback toward $60k?

The most recent CPI release has caused a strong risk-off move and pushed BTC down below $75k. While it is still only 4% from November open, the risk of further downside is increasing.

Technically, $75k is an important level, as it is a cost basis for short-term holders, meaning it could form a significant supply zone, with investors looking to sell at close to breakeven.

The recent Glassnode report adds another layer. As can be seen in the chart below, the largest Bitcoin liquidity cluster is concentrated around $60k. These are mostly longs opened at the bottom of the market, which haven’t been re-tested by BTC since it rallied from around $65k in August.

$60k is therefore a major downside liquidity cluster that is around 20% away from $75k.

The key takeaway? According to Glassnode analysts, a rally to $60k could result in large amounts of long-term liquidation. However, this can create an accumulation opportunity for patient accumulators, those who are looking to take advantage of the forced selling.

This certainly adds pressure at the $75k level. Technically, Bitcoin is still inside this range, and there isn’t much to lose here for bulls. But with how the recent CPI data has gone, it might be harder to stay above $75k.

The expectations for December rate cuts have changed, and the on-chain fundamentals are very bearish.

So, if there is not enough buy-side liquidity to absorb the sell-side liquidity around $75k, BTC could face a deeper correction as long liquidations accelerate. This could bring the $60k liquidity pocket into focus, where even bullish investors may find a better accumulation opportunity.

So, is Bitcoin [BTC] heading for a deeper retracement before its next recovery?

The broader liquidity crunch is now weighing on the Bitcoin network.

The stablecoin market has seen its quickest drop in supply since 2022, losing a total of $14.5 billion in 30 days. According to Glassnode, this is due to holders swapping their stablecoins back into dollars and taking funds out of crypto.

Tether alone accounted for $8.4 billion of the decline, more than half the total drop.

The result? As can be seen in the chart below, Bitcoin’s daily trading volume has dropped to a record low of 126k BTC in 2026. This is down from around 453k BTC in 2022. So, technically, there has been an overall decline of around 72% in Bitcoin’s spot trading volume.

The key takeaway? Glassnode says a return to supply growth would be the first sign that new money is coming in.

In essence, the decline in stablecoin and Spot volumes shows that there is more liquidity being drained from the market. This will leave Bitcoin much more vulnerable and will make it harder to hold the $75k level. In turn, the $60k region is looking like an area of accumulation, reinforcing Glassnode’s thesis.

Bitcoin risks a deeper drop toward $60k as selling pressure builds around $75k.

Stablecoin supply and BTC trading volume are falling, signaling less money flowing into the crypto market.

XRP sharks add 16.8K wallets as price nears critical $1 support: Can buyers defend it?

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Large holders are rapidly accumulating Ripple [XRP] as price slides toward the $1 psychological level.

Shark wallets, which hold 10K XRP tokens or above, have increased by over 16.81K over the last nine months, now at 343.5K. This growth is equivalent to 5.15%, indicating accumulation, but retailers are on the sidelines fearing the altcoin could dip below $1.

Over the past two months, XRP has lost about 30% in capitalization while whales and sharks continue to add more positions. This is a clear divergence showing that big investors are capitalizing on the fear to build discounted positions.

Despite this ongoing capital inflow, the altcoin continues to weaken as there is no cohesion among all XRP traders.

Meanwhile, the Estimated Leverage Ratio (ELR) of the XRP Ledger on the Binance exchange has dropped sharply from 0.2086 to 0.1437.

This is the lowest level the metric has reached over the past three months when the value was 0.14. What followed next was a dip to $0.9925, where it rallied thereafter as ELR increased.

This decline in ELR implies that derivatives risk exposure is reducing as XRP trades around $1.05. That is, either through closure of positions or increased collateral deposits.

Amidst a significant drop in systemic leverage risk and a more stable derivatives market environment, forced liquidations often follow. However, they could mark potential inflection points in the market structure.

On the charts, the altcoin is approaching this inflection point as the price nears $1. In mid-August, XRP rallied from this level to a high of $1.699 in just four days.

Consequently, XRP plunged below the true close at $1.55, and its second attempt to stay above level failed. Over the past two months, the altcoin has been in a constant decline.

This is clearly evident with the daily trading volume of the altcoin being dominated by bears. At the time of press, the spot volume for XRP/USDT on Binance was at $17 million.

Moreover, the RSI Divergence printed a bearish signal, reinforcing the price weakness.

However, the Net Volume was slowly rising from the negative territory and briefly surpassed the neutral level, now at 2.10 million tokens.

Therefore, if the 10K wallets remain in this aggressive growth and Bitcoin [BTC] stabilizes, a hold above $1 may be viable. Thereafter, XRP would reclaim $1.10 or $1.15, but losing the $1 support would create a short-term sell-off.

Mormon Wives' Aspyn Ovard Shares Daughter's Terminal Cancer Diagnosis

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

Aspyn Ovard shared during the premiere episode of Hulu's Secret Lives of Mormon Wives: Orange County that her and ex-husband Parker Ferris' daughter was diagnosed with terminal cancer.

Aspyn Ovard's family is navigating an unimaginable health journey.

The Secret Lives of Mormon Wives: Orange County star, mom to daughters Cove, 7, Lola, 5, and Elle, 2, with ex-husband Parker Ferris, shared that one of their kids has been diagnosed with terminal cancer.

"Everything happened so, just out of nowhere," Ovard said through tears during a confessional interview for the Hulu show, which premiered Nov. 12. "One day she was OK, and then the next day she wasn't."

"At the hospital, you're like, 'It has to be fine,'" the 30-year-old continued. "You can't fathom that they're actually going to tell you that your baby has cancer, and that it's terminal. She could be gone in a month, we don't know. I've been living like that for over three years, just having no idea."

As Ovard explained, her relationship with Ferris had already reached a breaking point prior to receiving the devastating health news.

"My marriage was nowhere near in a place to be able to make it through something like this," the YouTuber, who split from Ferris in 2024, noted. "We weren't even going to make it through before this happened."

"It was just so toxic and hard," she added. "And through all of it, I feel like I still tried to take care of him because I know how hard he's grieving."

During a therapy session with Ferris, Ovard—now part of a throuple with Bri Davis and her husband Billy Davis—explained to her ex that she's trying to make money so that they won't have to work if they're mourning the loss of a child.

"You know that I will always take care of you because I always have!" Ovard told Ferris during a disagreement. "When everything goes to s--t and things get really hard, I don't want you to have to do anything."

The former couple, high school sweethearts from Utah, tied the knot in October 2015 when she was 19 and he was 20.

Ovard filed for divorce from Ferris nearly nine years later, on April 1, 2024, the same day she announced the birth of their third daughter.

In September 2024, months after filing for divorce, Ovard revealed whether she'd ever consider walking down the aisle again.

"I don't think that I really believe in the concept of marriage at this point," she said on the Weekly Trash podcast at the time. "I would have a long-term partner if I met someone, and I feel like this is it. But to get legally married, I wouldn't ever do again. It's just too hard to separate."

"I think just legally, it's just too much to it," Ovard added. "Even the concept of marriage, you just never know what life is going to throw at you so I think to make those vows, I don't feel like I can really commit to that even if I loved someone so much."
